# UI grammar and glossary

One vocabulary for the door's screens. It was first checked against the code on `feature/door-writes`
(PIE-433) and is kept current with main: a PR that adds or changes a shared part updates its row here.
The line numbers in the citations below are from that first audit and drift; the
[architecture map](architecture/map.json) is the checked index (`bun scripts/architecture-map.ts --sync` in packages/door keeps its lines current). Use this page to name things in code,
READMEs and roadmap items, and to judge whether a new screen reuses the grammar or invents its own.

## Text must stay reachable

Table stakes for every surface (the panels capability, `056753fc`): wrap where the container can grow,
scroll when long, and keep the whole value when shortening is unavoidable. All shortening uses `pad`,
`drawEllipsized`, `rowLines` or `fitHint`, never a local slice and ellipsis. Keep status messages whole until
`App.flash`, so `status.list` and the message log retain them. The final composed frame owns reveal placement,
including text inside floats: hover or keyboard selection shows the full value; Alt+? cycles clipped values.
The one-line reveal scrolls if it is itself wider than the screen. Reading it never changes a click's action.

## Before adding a feature

Check here for the part that already does it. Skipping this step is how the door got two readers,
three layout models and four searches (§4).

| The feature needs to… | Use | Where |
|---|---|---|
| prove which code answered an action | `probe("name")` reads the dispatch request's `messageContext`; `ep0ch trace on/off/show` uses the existing control socket, keeps 100 runs beside the status log, and reports the door's startup revision. The service mirror reads change attribution. Dev-tool walkthrough: ep0ch-core's **Prove which code ran**; ownership contract: `test/parity-probes.test.ts`. | `src/surface/probe.ts`, `src/surface/dispatch.ts`, `src/trace.ts` |
| say what keys do: a hint row, a panel's footer, a strip under an attached surface, a card, a help screen, x-ray, the power bar | the **key table** that binds them: key → action as data, the handler dispatching from it (the `^W` table, `W_KEYS`, today, and the panel a pause after a prefix opens from it, every key grouped in its action's words: `desk/key-panel.ts`, PIE-1304; its first group the focused tile's own keys, its ⋯ menu's own rows (`Dispatcher.menu`, `MenuRow.own`: its kind's, a reader's note and list actions, not the screen's every-tile rows) that have a key, PIE-1338; PIE-1173 gives every mode and tile one, and `ActionDef.keys` becomes derived from the tables, since a free-text `keys` drifts too: `sources.close` declared an `esc` it doesn't bind); labels and hints from the `ActionDef`s through the dispatcher (`Dispatcher.defOf`); each moment says them in **one** place, the tile's hint row, and other places only point at it. A key list written as a string literal (`"j k select · ⏎ open · esc close"`) is a finding: it drifts from what the keys do, and two of them disagree on screen (PIE-1171: three lines, three wordings, one wrong about Esc). Esc's word comes from what Esc will close (the innermost open thing, the mode stack). An action's own keys are rows of keys and what each does (`ActionDef.keys`, `KeyRow`, PIE-1048; `test/key-rows.test.ts` refuses a line that doesn't read as keycaps). A key table says itself with `keyTable(scope, about, rows)` so the keys window lists it; every table is read through `effective` (the person's keymap file, `surface/keymap.ts`), and a hint row part that names an action is a `HintPart` drawn by `partsHint`, so a rebind shows wherever the key is said. Listing every binding with its scope, layer and shadows is the **binding catalog** (`surface/key-catalog.ts`), never a list written again. | `src/surface/actions.ts`, `src/surface/dispatch.ts`, `src/surface/modes.ts`, `src/desk/wkeys.ts`, `src/surface/keymap.ts`, `src/surface/key-catalog.ts`, `src/desk/keys-window.ts` |
| read a cut-short string in full | `clipText`, `pad`, `drawEllipsized`, `rowLines` and `fitHint` carry compact references through the canvas as zero-cell drawing marks. `ellipsize` and `shortId` return plain text for data and refusals. `Overflow` resolves the final composed cells, lazily caches a reveal of at most 4 KiB plus an omitted-character count, reveals hovered or selected values in one line and scrolls those wider than the screen. The value cache retains at most 1,024 source references. Hover reports (`?1003h`, a report for every cell the pointer crosses) are handled at most once a frame, the newest winning (`App.key`, PIE-1300), and `Overflow.hover` says whether the reveal changed so `text.hover` redraws only then; `peek` shows `hover` and `speed`. Alt+? (`text.reveal`) cycles the clipped values without changing focus. Sources headers and values wrap without a row cap; tables drop the id column before shrinking a title. A table cell wraps without a work-id hanging indent. Selecting a table or decision row reveals all its lines; its ▸, Enter and `figure.row` retain the expansion without moving another reader’s selection (PIE-1423). `status.list` also returns recent messages in full. | `src/style.ts`, `src/text.ts`, `src/hyperlink.ts`, `src/overflow.ts`, `src/canvas.ts`, `src/app.ts`; showcase `hints` |
| locate the tile an open landed in | the desk's shared tile frame draws an arrival accent in the bright tone with a `*` in its title, visible in peek; focus, scroll, a click or ten visible seconds acknowledge it. The layout module reconciles names across the screen and drawer; saved tiles retain their spec role when renamed. The wire card lists numbered screen targets. | `src/desk/desk.ts`, `src/desk/screen-layout.ts`; showcase `drawer` |
| draw anything pressable: a note's `::button`, a script view's button, an output region's, a proposal's or a thread's controls, the empty screen's, the status bar's; say it is running, done, failed or can't run now (PIE-1287) | **the button part** (`src/surface/button.ts`): `buttonRows(label, look, press)` (one row, or three with a frame) and `buttonText` (one row, the status bar's) draw outline-core's look words (`button-call.ts`: `glyph=`, `tone=` quiet/primary/danger as theme roles, `frame=` over the box glyph sets, `fill=`); `pressing` keeps a press's state per button (running once it takes a moment, done, failed with its words until the next press; a script run that outlasts the wait until `scripts.ended`, `pressEnded`), and disabled is the action's refusal now (`Dispatcher.refusedNow`, the checks a run makes, run nothing); under the pointer it is drawn lit by the one Overflow pass (`litText`), a failed or disabled one reading its why there. A `[label]` drawn by hand in a control is a finding. Pages draw the same look (`buttonHtml`, inert) | `src/surface/button.ts`, `outline-core/src/button-call.ts`, `src/overflow.ts`, `src/surface/dispatch.ts`, outliner `src/component-primitives.ts`; showcase `buttons` |
| put a note button on the status bar; order or hide its built-ins; group buttons in a menu; keep them on a narrow bar | `App.statusItems` declares each built-in's text, place, dispatcher bindings and its narrow words (`short`, the drawer chip's `tiny`). `readStatusNote` reads `[status-bar::true]` as a list (PIE-1224): `- <id>` places a built-in, a `::button` (on its own line or a list item: outline-core's button-call grammar reads both) presses through `button.press note=...`, `alt=` names an alternate, and an item with items under it is a group whose click is `status.bar.menu`, the **tile menu** (`tileMenu`, its `host` running rows on the App's dispatcher) over the screen. A line it can't read is skipped with a did-you-mean (`closest`), shown as one `! line N` item whose click opens the note there (a `Reveal`). The fit pass ranks items (`RANK`: pinned > listed > default, PIE-1213): built-ins shorten, then drop, then pins go into `+N`, which opens the same menu with what it hid. Drawing and presses share one hit-box table. `status.bar.items` describes the ActionDefs those bindings run, `status.bar.edit` opens the note (right-click/alt+click empty bar space), and `button.pin` is the note menu entry. A second `[status-bar::true]` note keeps today's bar. | `src/status-bar.ts`, `src/app.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts`; showcase `status-bar` |
| recover and search a status message after its flash | `App.flash` appends to the session's `StatusLog` (last 500 in memory, size-rotated JSONL under `outlineState()`). `StatusMessages` uses the BBS list actions, `RowView`, uncapped `rowLines`, `LineInput` and outline-core's search matcher. `status.log` opens it, `status.filter` searches it, `status.reload` refreshes its stable rows; Enter returns to the note or tile. Each message's words go through the reader's inline renderer (`presentLinks`): a labelled reference reads as its label and a click on it opens the note (`status.link`). A message the status bar cuts is said whole above it (`withWhole`, at most four rows, the fourth ending "… more alt+M" when it's longer); a click on those rows opens the log. Stacks stay in the file; `peek` carries messages; `ep0ch log --follow` tails the file. | `src/status-log.ts`, `src/app.ts`, `src/screens.ts`; showcase `message-log` |
| know how fast the door keeps up, and say so when it slips (PIE-1300, PIE-1309) | `SpeedMeter` (`src/speed.ts`, `App.speed`) counts frames, input reports and hover reports folded, and measures the settle: the last input of a burst to the frame that shows it, p50/p95/worst over the latest 200 bursts, for an input whose frame is drawn in the same turn. `describe()` gives it as `peek`'s `speed` (`ep0ch peek speed`); `slow()` is the status bar's `slow` chip (`App.statusItems`) once the last minute's p95 has stayed over 100 ms for a minute, a click on which is `status.speed`. Read the meter; never time a paint again somewhere else. | `src/speed.ts`, `src/app.ts`, `src/screens.ts`; `test/speed.test.ts`, `test/status.test.ts` |
| draw a tab set or a row of selectable chips | `tabStrip` (PIE-1288) is every tab set's one strip: the power bar's scopes, a tile's tab set, `::graph-tabs`, the library, Welcome, Tune and a form's choices, each in its own `style`; it returns rows with their click ranges (each an action, so a click and a key run the same one), and `tabKeys` the ModeKey rows (`by=±1`, digits). One overflow rule: `wrap`, or `share` (even shares, then "… N more", the lit tab kept). | `src/surface/chip-rows.ts`; showcase `style`, `library`, the power bar, panes |
| offer a first step in an empty screen, drawer or agent panel | `HomeSurface`, using the document renderer, the button part and `::graph-keys`; presses go through the existing actions | `src/surface/home.ts`, `src/desk/desk.ts`, `src/desk/agents-panel.ts`; showcase `drawer` |
| explain a mode or reveal a frame's controls without taking keys | `HintCard`, composed in the desk float layer with `Canvas.box`, `RowView` and the `graph-keys` keycaps. `ActionDef.hint` or a mode's card declares its words. `menuRow` formats the interaction choices; the dispatcher owns execution. `hint.show/get/pin/dismiss/never`, `hint.scroll`, `hints.list/set/reset` and `xray` share one ActionSet. The tune inspector's Hints section discovers ActionDef hints and the mode registry; overrides use the shared door state helpers. | `src/surface/hint-card.ts`, `src/desk/desk.ts`; showcase `hints` |
| reply to a note and read its conversation | `NoteSurface` owns the temporary split and source position; `DraftSession` with `noteReplyTarget` keeps a reply unsaved until submit. The existing editor draws it. `readReplies`, `linkRows` and `linkRowLines` share the children/replies group with the links model; the thread drawer is a reader mode. | `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/draft-session.ts`, `src/links.ts`; showcase `replies` |
| read a thread as a tree, and pick a post without leaving the one you're reading (PIE-944) | the reader's THREAD panel (`note.thread`, a reader mode): `readThread` (`src/links.ts`) reads the note's ancestors from the service once (`blocks.context`) and follows `replyTo` through them (a moved reply's parent is asked for singly), and, for each level, the replies the service lists for that parent (`readReplies`, the one membership query); `threadRows` lays them out as the links model's rows (`child` rows with `thread`, `fold` rows), `linkRowLines` draws them: the root, the parents down to the note (marked ►, its author and age on every row), the posts beside each, the note's replies; a busy level, a long chain and a long run of replies fold around the note with a count, and ⏎ (or a double click) on the fold shows them. A click selects, a double click or ⏎ opens (`RowPresses`, `RowPress.selected`); `T` is the full list, `b` the outlinks and backlinks | `src/links.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/scroll.ts` (`pressOn`); showcase `thread` |
| keep a reading line and move by blocks, sections or actions | `NoteSurface` uses its `ReaderPosition.reading` source offset and `Doc.source` for the whole wrapped block; the fixed gutter shares folds and comments. | `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/surface/selection.ts`; showcase `reading-line` |
| render or read a note | `NoteSurface`, hosted through `SurfaceHost` (a view with its own header gives its rows through `SurfaceHost.header`, as the BBS message reader does, PIE-426); the body drawn by `renderDoc` after `presentLinks`; a list item, a plain heading and a styled heading drawn plainly wrap through one hanging wrap (`hang` in `src/doc.ts`, PIE-1349: the rows, a copy's cuts and joins, and a fold's count kept inside the width), and a wrapped heading's disclosure is every row it takes (`Doc.heads[].rows`); every child document (box, callout, output, rule frame, embedded note or list preview) takes `childEnv` (`src/doc.ts`), which keeps hooks in the owning note's line coordinates and scopes its header image; `expandsEmbeds` (`src/embed-policy.ts`) decides whether the surface has an outline embed renderer, including new-note and new-card drafts; literal regions (PIE-422) and code fences found by outline-core's `code-ranges.ts` and `code-fence.ts`, and links (`((id\|label))`, `[[page\|label]]`, embeds, Markdown links) and fragment anchors by its `link-syntax.ts`, the service's own rules, `presentLinks` splicing each link in at its offsets; inline Markdown (PIE-444: bold, italic, strikethrough) by `src/inline.ts` (checked against marked, Detail's parser, and parity-tested); Markdown links open through `src/open.ts`; the inline links component (`::links`, `::outlinks`, `::resources`, `::backlinks`, one line with a filter or a block to its `::` with `of:` `filter:` `title:` `groups:`) by `renderLinkBlock` in `src/links.ts`, the links tile's rows in a figure's frame, each tagged through `DocEnv.tag` so `[ ]`, ⏎ and a click open it; transclusions (`!((id))`, `!((id^fragment))`) by `src/embeds.ts` from the service's projection (`transclusions.read`, PIE-424: fragment slices, nesting depth, cycles and their wording are the service's), each embedded body drawn by the surface's own renderer; a followed `((id^fragment))` revealed from `fragments.read` (PIE-425). Whether an outline event means the note shown must be read again is `NoteSurface.staleOn` (every host asks it: the desk's and the board's readers, the river's columns, the BBS message reader) and reads it again by `NoteSurface.reread` (one read at a time: a burst of changes makes one more, not one each), so a note trashed or restored with an ancestor says so. What takes a reader's keys besides reading is a **mode** in its mode stack (PIE-516, `src/surface/modes.ts`: a step's status choice, the property panel, the edit, the comment session; each with key, click, press, wheel, rows, leave, holdsKeys, describe), their precedence stated once (`PRECEDENCE`); `holdsKeys`, `choosing`, `editing`, `sessionOf`/`sessionWord` (what hosts name and track) and what's drawn in place of the note derive from it. The edit and the comment draw in place of the note; the panel and the status choice are drawn inside it by the reading render. A new thing that takes a reader's keys is a mode, never another field the surface checks; reading itself (layout, elements, folds, history, selection, projections) stays the surface's, and it stays the only reader. Outside the door, `ep0ch show <id>…` (`src/notes-cli.ts`) draws notes through this same surface for a program, its live figures and `::links` connected to the outline as the App connects them (`connectFigures`, `src/graphs.ts`): plain, `--ansi`, or `--cells` (`src/cells.ts`), the cell grid the Claude mod's BlockView paints as a `Raster`; never a second renderer (`--source` prints the text as written, no drawing). | `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/doc.ts`, `src/refs.ts`, outline-core `src/code-ranges.ts`, `src/inline.ts`, `src/open.ts`, `src/embeds.ts`, `src/notes-cli.ts`, `src/cells.ts` |
| keep another outline's note on this screen | `OutlineBoards` owns one connection per outline and machine for the door; desk readers and EchoNet share it. `Msg.address` carries door-local provenance through navigation; the reader's `outline` and `machine` spec fields restore its board and `DraftSession`. `NoteSurface` and its live figures read that board; the frame names a foreign outline. `open` accepts an ep0ch URI, `tile.open` takes `outline=`/`machine=`, and the note picker accepts `outline:<name> words`. Blockdown links accept `[[outline:page]]`, `((outline:id|label))` and `!((outline:id^anchor))`; a bare UUID missing locally resolves across the host's outlines and forwards. The service returns the address; `NoteSurface` draws its outline and opens the existing reader tile. The `[[` completer offers outline names ending in `:`, then their pages. | `src/desk/outline-boards.ts`, `src/desk/panes.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts`; showcase `cross-outline` |
| open one addressed block as the whole screen (`ep0ch --screen detail <ep0ch://…>`) | the **detail screen**: the `detail` screen spec, whose one tile is a reader (a `reader` tile: the same `NoteSurface`) on the note its `note` arg names; `--screen detail` resolves a canonical URI to its outline and machine first (`screenRequest` in `src/start.ts`), never a second reader | `src/desk/screen-specs.ts`, `src/desk/builtin-tiles.ts`, `src/start.ts`, `src/main.ts` |
| scroll a view (the wheel, PgUp PgDn); press a list's row (select, open, open beside); fold the notes under a row | `src/scroll.ts`: `scrolled(top, by, max)` moves a position by rows within its content and nothing else; `wheelRows(dir)` is what one wheel report scrolls (`EP0CH_SCROLL_ROWS`, default 1, no timing: the terminal already paces a trackpad's reports); `RowView`, every list's cursor and scroll (a thread's replies, the welcome's notes, a lane's cards, the tree, the links tile, the board's steps; the selection comes into view only when it moved or the view's height changed, never on a repaint; a selection several rows tall names its rows; `reveal` brings it back after the wheel), never a list's hand-rolled `if (sel < top) top = sel` (a text cursor, the editor's or a passage's, follows its own). A press uses `RowView.press` / `RowPresses`: any ordinary single click selects (even on the selected row), a double click opens, alt-click alt-opens. The BBS lists' `Pointer` uses the same gesture decision. Menus and launchers may explicitly act on one click. The sideways wheel (`wheel-left`, `wheel-right`, SGR 66 and 67) is never the vertical one: `SidewaysWheel` makes a swipe's burst one step, and a tile kind's `sideways` says what a step means where its content is horizontal (the river's columns; the board's lanes take it as h l), elsewhere nothing. A list whose rows open to show the notes under them (the outline tree's children, a river column's replies) keeps which are open and those children in a `Fold` (`src/fold.ts`: read on the first open, and again when the outline changes them: `reread` the open notes, `shows` says whether a change touches a row; walked depth first), never its own `open` set and `kids` map. A list whose wheel moves its selection moves it one row a report. A frame of nothing but wheel reports is `onlyScrolled()`, and a reader moves its last layout (`NoteSurface.layOut`) instead of laying the note out again. Past the end (PIE-622): a reader or a draft scrolls until its last row is `overscrollRows` above the bottom edge (`lastTop`; `reader.overscroll half\|none\|<rows>`, half by default: the middle), and End, `G` and `scroll to=end` stop first where it's on the edge (`endTop`), then go on; a draft following its cursor keeps `scrollOff` rows round it (`Draft.render`). NoteSurface (with its header image's give-back), a river column and a draft all clamp by them, never a `total - room` of their own | `src/scroll.ts`, `src/app.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/edit.ts` |
| name a shortcut for another action with arguments bound (a lift of the drawer's agent, a button's act that deserves a name, a row in the power bar, a page in `actions`) | a **preset** (`preset()`, `src/surface/actions.ts`, PIE-1279): `{ to: { action, tile?, args }, first: [steps…], then: [steps…] }` (`then` runs after the target and reads its answer) plus its own summary, keys and the arguments a caller may change; it runs the target through the dispatcher (`Again`), so the target's rules, tile grammar and attribution hold. `agent.lift` is the first. | `src/surface/actions.ts`, `src/drawer.ts` |
| let a person or agent do anything; let an agent see what the person sees; document an action (its page) | the action registry: an `ActionDef` in an `ActionSet` (`actionSet<Host>()(scope, { name: def({ …, args, run }) })`: its argument types come from its `args` schema, `ArgsOf`, never written twice); the key, the mouse and `act` all call it; an action has one name and one def (a change that isn't an action is a bug, and `test/parity.ts`, run by `test/parity-*.test.ts`, finds it: it presses every key and clicks across every screen, traces the actions run with `traceActions`, and checks each key against the def's `keys` and each hint against `declaredKeys`, PIE-506). The shell's `SHELL_ACTIONS` (`screen.open`, `screen.back`, `screen.list`, `screen.help`, `video.cycle`, reached from a screen screens.ts imports through `src/shell-keys.ts`, and `screen.shell`, drop to shell: the person's only, through `App.suspend` as ctrl+e's `$EDITOR`, `src/drop.ts`) work on every screen and the BBS lists' `LIST_ACTIONS` (`list.select`, `list.open`, `list.read`) on each list (PIE-489); an agent's move of the person's screen is an action that declares `touches: "screen"`, checked by the dispatcher (next row). Reads: `layout.get`, `view.get`; the live feed: `subscribe` on the control socket, diffed from the screen's `viewState()` after each paint (`App.subscribe`). See [the agent interface](AGENT-INTERFACE.md) Running one: the screen host's **dispatcher** (PIE-514, `Dispatcher` in `src/surface/dispatch.ts`): a screen registers its action sets (its own, `TILE_ACTIONS`, a tile kind's, `NOTE_ACTIONS` on a reader, `EXT_ACTIONS`) and says what its tiles are (`TileRef`: name, id, number, aliases, what each shows) and where the person's keys are on it (`Screen.keys`); the dispatcher reads `tile=` by one grammar, finds which set owns the action, checks `expected=`, checks the actor against the action's declared `touches` (`actorRule`, against the shell's one **whereabouts** query, `App.person()` in `src/whereabouts.ts`), runs it, says a refusal, repaints for the person's keys once (an action that already redrew itself, or that declares it changed nothing shown, gets no second paint: `ActionDef.repaints`, PIE-1301), and flashes the def's `says`. Keys and clicks call `press`/`pressIn`, the control socket `act`. A screen in a tile (a board on a desk) is reached through a delegation, its whereabouts seen from inside (`within`). Never a screen-local `act`, a tile-name parser, or an `actor.kind` check for the person's keys: declare `touches` (`nothing`, `tile`, `shape`, `draft`, `screen`), `while`, `draft`, `person` (the person's own: their press's run may), `personAlways` (theirs alone, never lifted: not by a press, not by yolo), `personWith` (when the arguments decide it) and `replay` on the def. A step inside an action that moves something of the person's asks `Dispatcher.rule(touches, actor, { tile, way, host })`, a layout context `layoutStanding`, a reader that may take another note `ReaderPane.keepsOff` (its words `holdsEdit`), a write who it's recorded as `recorded(actor)` in `src/socket.ts`: one owner each (PIE-1294), and `test/one-owner.test.ts` fails when a file decides one itself **An action's page** (PIE-1032): the **action page** (`actionPage` in `src/surface/action-page.ts`): Blockdown written from the `ActionDef` alone, drawn by the readers' renderers (`draftPreview`): the lead (the summary to its first `.:;(`), its keys as a `::graph-keys` (every way to press it, `keyAlternatives` in `src/figures/keys.ts`, the keycaps `keycaps` draws), its examples as a `::graph-example` (the def's `examples`: one to three act lines and what each does, `safe` read from `touches: nothing` + `replay: safe` unless the example says; an action with arguments has them, `test/action-pages.test.ts` checks each is a line `act` takes), its arguments as a `::graph-props` (the **properties table**, `drawProps` in `src/figures/props.ts`: the library's component pages draw theirs with it, and an extension's page its actions' arguments, outliner `actionArgTables`), then the rest of the summary a fact a line. Never a paragraph of keys and arguments, a second keycap drawing or a table of a page's own | `src/surface/actions.ts`, `NOTE_ACTIONS` in `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/control.ts`, `src/app.ts`, `docs/AGENT-INTERFACE.md`, `src/surface/dispatch.ts`, `src/whereabouts.ts`, `src/app.ts` (`person`), `test/dispatch.test.ts`, `src/surface/action-page.ts`, `src/figures/props.ts`, `src/figures/example.ts`, `src/actions-doc.ts` |
| see and change an extension's settings (its config.json) without opening the file (PIE-1343) | **the action form over the manifest's configSchema**: the service reads each extension's settings against its schema (`extensions.list`'s `settings`: the file on the host, each field, the state default/set/missing/failing) and writes them (`extensions.configure`, checked against the schema, outliner `src/extension-settings.ts`); the door binds `ext.<id>.settings`, an action whose `ArgSpec`s are the schema's fields (`settingArgs`, the only new part), so its form is the one every action with arguments opens (`openActionForm`, `askInBar`); the power bar's `&` shows the state on the row and a `settings <name>` row (preview and ⏎ the form, filled in) | `src/extension-settings.ts`, `src/bar/sources.ts` (`EXTENSIONS`), `src/bar/form.ts`, outliner `src/extension-settings.ts`; showcase `ext-settings` |
| refer to a tile, pane, note, outline or session from code (a follower, a link between tiles, a saved reference, a pane to close later) | **an id or a role, never a name as a stand-in for one** ([ADR 0001](../../../docs/adr/0001-ids-names-roles.md)): keep the tile id, block uuid, Herdr pane id or pid; or a role re-resolved at use (`from=$EP0CH_TILE`, the drawer's agent, `[welcome::n]`). A name (a tile name, a pane label, a title) is what a person types and what the screen shows: the dispatcher looks it up in one scope and refuses an ambiguous one with the candidates' ids. A name derived from user data never equals a reserved one (prefix or hash it) | `docs/adr/0001-ids-names-roles.md`, `src/surface/dispatch.ts` (`TileRef`) |

A mount is **named for what it mounts** (`tile.open kind=screen screen=who` is `who`, a part is named for the part, a group `group`; a second is numbered), and the person's click, key and right-click on a tile in a mount go to the dispatcher **by the tile's id** (`tileMenu` passes `tileId`), so two tiles named `reader` in different layers never refuse the person: only an agent's `tile=reader` is refused, naming the paths (`calls/reader`) or ids to use. **Per instance**: each mount's layout, focus, spines, selection, scroll and a lane's cursor, saved as its tile spec's `inner` in the layout of the screen holding it (`DeskOpts.saved`, `onSave`: never the full screen's file; a whole screen's first mount starts as its full screen was last left, read only); its terminals are kept under `<home>:<mount id>:<tile id>` (`Desk.home`). **Shared**: the outline (the hub, its views, the cards, drafts held on the service). `mount.out` (`^W u`, `▲ full` on its header) opens the full screen over this one (`Desk.poppedFrom`), its own instance; `q` or `screen.mount` there comes back to the mount where it was. `screen.mount` (`^W M`) and `screen.part` (`^W I`, the tile menu's "put lanes on the desk") put a full screen, or the part around a tile, on the desk with its arrangement. **No loops**: a mount is refused when its screen is in the chain of screens above it, or holds one that is (its saved spec is walked), or would nest past `MAX_MOUNT_DEPTH` (4); the refusal is the chain (`daily-test → focus → daily-test`, `mountProblem` in `src/desk/screen-spec.ts`, asked by `tile.open`, by `mountDesk` on restore, where the mount comes back a place holder saying why, and by a saved screen's spec loading). Never a second click system, a second history or a second place a drawing lives | `src/components.ts`, `src/projection.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts` (`runPress`, `keptDrawings`), `src/surface/invite.ts`, `src/surface/txn.ts`, `src/desk/layout-history.ts`, `src/desk/desk.ts`, `src/edit.ts`; the press showcase |
| draw a layout change as movement, and keep what a control command reads settled (PIE-958) | **`src/motion.ts`** is the one engine (pure): `planMotion(hint, mode)` is what the person's `motion` setting (`full` \| `calm` \| `off`, `motion.set`, `EP0CH_MOTION`) leaves of an actor's `motion=` hint (the dispatcher-reserved argument, `src/surface/txn.ts`), `Transition` holds the tiles' rectangles before and after, and `inFlight` is where one is drawn on the way (a plain ease, or fantasia's bent path), whole cells, inside the screen. `Desk.startMotion` is called where the layout history records a change (`recordChange`) and where an undo or an early end puts it back (`rebuildTo`): it captures the rectangles drawn now (or the in-flight ones, if a change is still going) and the next `render` starts the transition from the new ones; `Desk.tick` asks for a frame while it runs; the tiles that moved are drawn last as a frame and a name (the drag ghost's drawing, steady colour, nothing blinks, inverts or fades, pictures under them are taken away) and land where the layout is. It is paint only: `hits`, `layoutGet`, `peek`, `viewState` and snapshots are the settled layout (`App.catchUp` calls the screen's `settle` before a control command reads), and `Desk.key` settles first so a click lands where the tile is. A drag's own steps are not animated (the pointer is the motion). Never a second place a tile's rectangle is decided | `src/motion.ts`, `src/desk/desk.ts` (`startMotion`, `settle`, `render`, `tick`), `src/app.ts` (`catchUp`), `src/screens.ts` (`motion.set`); the motion showcase |
| run a whole choreography from one button, stop it with Esc or a click, and take it back in one step (PIE-959) | **the chain, not a new part**: a `::button{… of=fantasia …}` (outline-core `button-call.ts`) is a press (`NoteSurface.runPress`) that invites the run (`src/surface/invite.ts`); the `fantasia` script's `act()` calls are one layout transaction (`txn=<run id>`, `connectScriptActions`) with `motion=` read from `props.motion` (`lineAction`), drawn as movement (`src/motion.ts`), closed by `scripts.ended` or put back if it ended early (`Desk.endTxn`), and taken back whole by `layout.undo`. **Esc** (while `Desk.runningScripts()` is not empty and nothing else holds the key), a click on the status bar's "■ stop the dance" (`Screen.dancing`) and `act layout.stop` are one action, `Desk.stopRuns` (`scripts.stop`). The invitation lifts the hold on the person's tile and on navigation for layout and tile actions only (`Person.invited`, `Whereabouts.navHold`), never for the tile they type in or for leaving a section. Never a second way to run, stop or undo a script's layout changes | `src/desk/desk.ts` (`runningScripts`, `stopRuns`, `endTxn`, `undoLayout`), `src/surface/dispatch.ts` (`actorRule`), `src/desk/screen-layout.ts`, `src/app.ts` (the chip), `src/showcase/seed.ts` (`FANTASIA`); the fantasia showcase |
| learn the scripts surface from notes you can paste and run (PIE-982) | **the scripts reference** (outliner `docs/scripts.md`) holds every global, function, `tools.*` and `view.*` entry with a `path:line` pointer, generated or checked against the code by `scripts/scripts-doc.ts`; its worked examples are fenced notes that `scriptExamples` (outliner) parses, so the page, the showcase seed and `test/scripts-docs.test.ts` share one source. No second copy of a script's surface in a skill or a README: link the page. | outliner `docs/scripts.md`, `src/script-examples.ts`, `scripts/scripts-doc.ts`; the `script-examples` showcase |

| keep the page still between reading and editing | `NoteSurface.position` is one source position: reading offset, source line at a screen row, and edit cursor. `PositionRows` projects `Doc.source` or Draft's wrap map into scroll rows; raw and rich edit use that same position through `DraftSession`. A held edit keeps its viewport on focus; a second click places the cursor. `PaneView.scroll` carries the editor's scroll bar too. | `src/reader-position.ts`, `src/surface/note.ts`, `src/edit.ts`, `src/draft-session.ts`; the `one-place` showcase |
| read a note’s front matter | `frontMatterLines` in outline-core bounds the title and first paragraph at the first blank line, a rule line (`---`) or a list item with no property in it (PIE-970: tags written under a rule are body text); the service’s property records supply scope, the shared list grammar supplies values, and `PropertyPanel` groups block properties separately from inline and line properties. Showcase: `front-matter`. | `outline-core/src/header-line.ts`, `outline-core/src/property-grammar.ts`, `src/surface/props-panel.ts` |

| hand a file to ep0ch from a shell, claude.ai or a phone (PIE-1267) | **the artifacts inbox and its watcher** (PIE-1008) install it; **upload-tokens.ts** owns the one-time lease and the device token (the lease pattern of `loki_lease`); **publish-upload.ts** is the one token route on the signed-in origin; the card is a day-note child found by `pages.follow`, drawn by `artifactCard`; the shell ActionSet exposes `artifact.lease` (`> upload` in the power bar). New: only the glue between them. |
| take a consistent SQLite copy for analysis | **ShareSessions** owns the one-time token, expiry and revocation; **OutlineSnapshots** owns the checked copy and machinery record; the publisher streams it; the shell ActionSet exposes `outline.snapshot` in the power bar, Tree/Detail their System actions | outliner `src/outline-snapshots.ts`, `src/share-sessions.ts`, `src/publish.ts`; door `src/screens.ts` | the `snapshot` showcase section |

- **Don't copy the parallel versions:** the `colourBody` bodies left in the desk's search preview and
  the river's cards (F1), the river's pane code (F2), the extra searches (F6).
- **Don't re-derive meaning the service owns** (view membership, property parsing, query evaluation,
  what a write into a view must change, backlinks, what changed); never compute it here. The door and the
  service speak one protocol (`PROTOCOL`, outline-core) and the door refuses a service on another. Where a call per paint is too
  slow, import the rule from outline-core (`@ep0ch/outline-core/property-grammar`), never a copy of it.
- **If the part doesn't exist yet or doesn't fit:** extend it, or write down why not in the PR.
  A new shared part gets a row here in the same PR.
- **Every row has a showcase section** (`scripts/try-it.sh --showcase`, PIE-439), in this table's
  order: `SECTIONS` in `src/showcase/showcase.ts`, drawn by the part itself. A new row gets its section
  and the seed content it needs (`src/showcase/seed.ts`) in the same PR, and a parallel version shown
  beside it until consolidation removes it.
- Name things with the glossary's canonical words (§1).

List tiles declare `previews: true` on their tile kind to show select, open and alt wires.
The tree, links, query, what-changed, waiting-on-you and river column (including the library) use Desk routing;
figure rows and the thread panel use the containing reader tile's wires through NoteSurface.
Drawer tiles link to each other on the drawer's own desk, or across its edge to a screen tile. Groups keep these wires when moved into the drawer and across screens; a temporarily absent screen target waits at its @screen path. Existing targets and a reader's links list are found inside groups too. Revealing one reveals its holding groups through the layout module's reveal operation. In the drawer, tile.collapse puts the drawer away or shows the named tab; host.toggle and tile.collapse also work from ::button.
Waiting-on-you's terminal rows select before Enter takes the keys to that terminal; its held-note rows use note wires.


## TL;DR

- **One reader.** Board, desk, river and the BBS message reader (`MessageReader` `scr:273`, PIE-426)
  host `NoteSurface`; the BBS one keeps its header through `SurfaceHost.header`. `colourBody` still draws
  the desk's search preview and the river's cards (lists, not readers).
- **Tile operations exist twice.** The desk and the board are one engine (PIE-511): the board is a screen spec
  on the desk (PIE-515), its lanes query tiles in a columns container, its docks and floats the desk's, its keys
  a key map naming the desk's tile actions (`x`, `o`, `T`, `B`, `{ }`). The river is one too (PIE-515): its columns
  are tiles in a flow, its `w` `p` `x` and back and forward the engine's tile actions.
- **One dispatcher per screen host (PIE-514).** Keys, clicks and `act` all run actions through it; `tile=` has
  one grammar everywhere, and whether an agent may is read from each action's declared `touches` against one
  whereabouts query, not decided screen by screen.
- **One owner for where a key goes (PIE-1438).** The person's whereabouts say which layer takes the next key: the
  door's own over everything (`over`, top first: the power bar, the key probe, a status-bar menu over the drawer), the drawer or the
  screen (`keys`), and a program under them (`program`, with whether something is open over it). The raw sink, the key
  router and Esc's hold read that answer; a new overlay is over a program by being a layer it reads (a screen pushed, an
  overlay on a desk), never by a check in the router. `test/one-owner.test.ts` fails on a new decider.
- **Agents can arrange the desk and the board.** Note actions are shared everywhere, and the `tile.*`
  actions (open, close, resize, zoom, float, pin, collapse) are shared by the desk
  and the board. The river has
  its own column actions. The BBS message reader has the note actions and its own `message.*`; the
  menu and every screen have the shell's `screen.*`, and the BBS lists `list.*` (PIE-489).
- **Words collide.** "Reader", "pin", "preview", "detail", "region" and "spine" each mean two
  things, and the door's "detail" is the outliner's *Current*, not its *Detail*.
- **Entity navigation is still in several places.** Backlinks are one drawing (`src/backlinks.ts`) in the
  board's dock, the backlinks tile, the welcome and the tree's `L`, which also lists a row's outlinks and
  resources (PIE-324's Tree view). Readers have back and forward (PIE-453). Children still appear in three
  shapes, and there is no connections screen (PIE-432).
- **Duplicates.** Four searches, two Who's Online views, two activity views. The BBS `Search`
  screen (`scr:338`) is unreachable.

## 1. Glossary

**Wires and the pin (PIE-1022, PIE-1207).** A source tile has three verbs: **select →**, **⏎ →** and **alt →**.
Frames show their targets; a select with no target says **nowhere**. There is no current note: an open lands where
a wire, the person's own target or `placement()` puts it. A reader is **▣ pinned** or not; pinned, it keeps its
note and every open aimed at it lands beside it. Wires belong to screen-layout; `tile.link verb=select|open|alt` changes them.


**Canonical** is the word to use from now on. **Aliases** are what the code or README also
calls it. **Tree/Detail** is the outliner's equivalent (or "none").

### Shell

| Canonical | Meaning | Code | Tree/Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| shell | Screen stack, status bar, key routing, agent entry | `App` `app:72` | Herdr |
| gutter | Fixed reader edge for thread marks and fold controls, independent of authored spacing | `NoteSurface`, `Desk` | comment controls |
| screen | One full-canvas mode on the stack | `Screen` `app:42` | a Pi pane |
| menu | The screen that opens other screens | `MainMenu` `scr:132` | `?` action list |
| status bar | Last row: screen, host:workspace, offline, video | `app:242` | Herdr frame title |
| flash | A 4-second message in the status bar | `flash` `app:116` | expiring receipt |
| Esc | **Closes the innermost temporary thing, and never leaves the screen or logs off.** Innermost first: what holds the keys (a picker or menu, the tile menu `⋯` too; a popup or overlay that takes keys; the keys box, `? more`; a pending chord, `^W`; link mode, `alt+l`; a search or filter being typed; an edit, with nothing typed, or with unsaved text `Esc` twice, PIE-475), then the drawer when it's up and the person is out of it, then what's lit in the tile the keys are in (a link or element, a selection, a tint), then the screen's own (a zoom, a dock the keys are in or one sliding over, the keys back home). A float keeps the keys (PIE-1418: given back to the tile under it they went somewhere the float hid, and the river's `x` then closed a column), so in one with nothing left Esc says `nothing to close · x closes the float` (`^W x` on a screen without the `x` key). With nothing left it does nothing and says `nothing to close · q leaves` on the focused tile's frame and the status bar (a refusal, PIE-727: again, it stays up, loud). Leaving a screen is `q` (`screen.back`) and the menu's own keys; quitting is `G` or `ctrl+c`. Typing in a terminal (a terminal tile, the drawer's agent), Esc is the program's, but while the power bar or a menu is over it (a status-bar group's, a tile's ⋯) the door reads it and that closes, and a second Esc within `ESC_SPENT_MS` (0.6 s) is held back and said, never the program's (PIE-1431) | `nothingToClose` in `src/shell-keys.ts`; the desk's chain in `Desk.keyIn`; the raw sink and `heldEsc` in `src/app.ts` | `Esc` closes Detail's editor |
| drawer | Your drawer, the host layer's (PIE-513, PIE-498): tabs of tiles that belong to the App, not a screen, pulled up over (or beside) any screen from the status bar; its agents are ordinary terminal tabs with role `agent` (PIE-1331). **Drawer** means only this: a tile put in it travels with the person (`tile.drawer`, `^W a`), an agent tab too | `AgentDrawer` on `hostLayer`, its desk `hostSpec` (`src/drawer.ts`) | a Herdr pane |
| power bar | One palette over every screen and the drawer (PIE-656): `ctrl+k`, the status bar's `^K`; a list of rows from its sources (tiles, notes, actions, recent, screens, an extension's), scoped by a prefix or alt+← alt+→, the lit one read on the right; ⏎ goes (an action that takes arguments: its form, a field per argument with the act line under them), alt+⏎ is the alternate, esc puts it away. "Palette" and "cmd-k" mean it | `PowerBar` (`src/bar/bar.ts`), `BarSource` (`src/bar/source.ts`), `BAR_ACTIONS` | Herdr's cmd-k (panes, not tiles) |
| bar source | What a power bar scope lists, previews and does: rows for a query, a preview, a pick, through the shared paths. The door's own (`%` tiles, `/` notes, `>` actions, `+` recent, `:` screens, `@` agents: an ask line, its prefix kept on the line, `inline`) and an extension's `bar[]` | `registerBarSource` (`src/bar/source.ts`), `src/bar/sources.ts`, outliner `extensions.bar` | none |
| action form | An action's arguments as fields in the power bar (PIE-1031): the ones it needs first, then `tile=` (the selector, not an argument), then the rest, each with what it means and its values (its `ArgSpec`'s `choices`, true or false, its `kind`'s values: tiles, notes, screens, views, properties); the act line it will run under them. It is the one action-argument prompt (`askInBar`, the `ask` scope), never a widget of the bar's own | `openActionForm` (`src/bar/form.ts`), `registerArgKind` (`src/bar/arg-kinds.ts`), `bar.form` | a note's `[form-field::]` form (PIE-1212), drawn in place by NoteForm |
| drop to shell | The BBS's drop to DOS: the door steps aside for the person's login shell and comes back where it was on `exit` (`screen.shell`, the person's only); in a session, in the terminal of the client with the person's keys | `screen.shell` in `SHELL_ACTIONS`, `runLoginShell` and `Handover` (`src/drop.ts`), `App.suspend` | none |
| session | The door kept running without a terminal (PIE-418): one per outline (`src/session/place.ts`), holding the screens, layouts, drafts and terminal tiles; ended only by `session.end` (`E`, `ep0ch session end`) | `src/session/daemon.ts` | a Herdr session |
| client, attach, detach | A terminal showing a session: `ep0ch` attaches (starting the session when none runs), quitting detaches (`G`, `ctrl+c`, a closed terminal); several at once, the keys wherever the person last typed; `--watch` is read-only | `src/session/client.ts`, `SessionTerm` | Herdr's attach, `ctrl+b q` |
| nest | The layers a program runs in, outermost first (`ssh:pts/5 › door:<pid>/desk/t1:claude`), each appended by the layer that starts the next; `ep0ch where` checks each | `EP0CH_NEST`, `src/nest.ts`, `src/where.ts` | none |
| chip | A label on the status bar that stands for the drawer (`▲ claude +2`: the tab shown, two tiles more); a click pulls it up; a tile dragged onto it goes in as a tab (it lights up: `into your drawer: travels with you`) | `AgentDrawer.chip` | none |
| hint row | Key help above the status bar | `dsk:210` `del:1450` `riv:397` | menu row |
| workspace | The outline connected to (one per run) | `ctx.workspace`, `--ws` | workspace |
| video mode | Kitty+CRT, Kitty, cells (`V`) | `cycleVideo` `app:118` | none |

### Layout: block, tile, container, screen

The layout vocabulary, settled on Oct 1. The design is the outline note "Screens are layouts: one tile system"
(linked from PIE-493 and PIE-498). New code and docs use these four words. Where the code still says
pane, region or column, the table below says what it is today.

| Word | Meaning |
|---|---|
| block | Content in the outline. It is data, never UI. |
| tile | A UI leaf that shows one thing: a block, a query, a terminal, a component or a tree. Its kind comes from the tile-kind registry (`src/desk/tile-kinds.ts`), where built-ins and extensions register the same way. |
| container | Holds tiles or other containers and decides how they're arranged: `split` (side by side or stacked, resizable), `tabs` (stacked, one visible), `columns` (side by side in the order their source gives, each its own weight), `dock` (slides out over the others from an edge and takes drops; `Dock` in `src/desk/layout.ts`, PIE-505), `flow` (columns where opens go into the next one, each full, peek or spine around the wide one; the river's; `Flow`, PIE-513). All five are built; the river screen ports onto `flow` next (PIE-515). |
| policy | What a container allows, saved with the screen: draggable, droppable, closable, `accepts: [tile kinds]`, resizable (`min`, `max`, `fixed` cells), collapsible, overlay (and its edge), `locked`, `opens-into` (`opensInto`: a tile, or a container's key where opens land in tiles opened there), `spines` (`centre` balances the stacks with an odd spine right; `book` by default: pushed pages stay on their own side, newest nearest the open tiles; `start` left, `end` right, `invoker` toward the opener), `min-width` (40 by default, 0 never folds: opening a spine shares the open group evenly and folds the least recently touched page when needed), `readable` (a flow's full column width; `room` only squeezes flows), `shuts` (a close in it shuts its dock), `opens` (the open rule: `current`, or `next` for a flow, a flow's own when it says none). The screen has one too, outermost: `locked` there is the screen's lock (the `▣ screen locked` chip, `layout.lock`); your drawer's desk and a group have their own, each named in what it says (PIE-1346). `Policy`, `effective` in `src/desk/layout.ts`, checked inside every operation of the screen-layout module; set by `layout.policy` or `^W P`. |
| host layer | Above every screen, kept across screen switches (Evan, Oct 1): the agent, the admin outline and detail, terminals; later a shelf for bundles in transit. A layout like any other, changed by the same operations: one slot for the screen shown (`HOST_SCREEN`) beside a real dock holding your drawer's tabs. The person's keys move through the drawer's own transitions (pulled up by the person: to its tab; put away: back to the screen as they left it). The screen's policy `host` says where it may appear: `beside` (the screen narrower or shorter), `over` (over its lower rows, the default), `none` (a full-screen screen). The drawer's verbs are `host.*` (`host.toggle`, `host.size`, `host.enter` into the tab it shows); the agent's own are `agent.*` (`agent.type`, `agent.restart`, `agent.knows`). `hostLayer`, `placeHost` in `src/desk/screen-layout.ts`. |
| screen layer | The screen shown (board, desk, river, reader, LORD): swapped per screen, each as small as it was designed. |
| layout operation | One change to a screen's layout, applied for one actor as one transaction by the screen-layout module: the new state or a refusal with its reason, nothing done (PIE-513). The only way a layout changes; keys, clicks and `act` reach it through actions. `Op`, `apply` in `src/desk/screen-layout.ts`. |
| screen | A **screen spec** on the desk, the one screen host (PIE-515): its containers and tiles, a key map naming actions, a hint, a band, where opens land. Board, desk, river, welcome, waiting and brief are all screens; the desk, the board, the welcome, waiting, the brief and a pinned page are specs (the river too, since PIE-515), plain data a note can hold. A screen's own layout between runs is kept in the door's state (`desk.json`, `delivery.json`, §3's persist row); a screen saved by name is a **screen note** in the outline (PIE-565, `[type::screen]`), which every door on it opens. |
| spine | A tile folded to a strip of its title (a column a few cells wide), still in the layout: click or open it and it unfolds. The open tiles and their spines read like an open book: the pages you're reading in the middle, the rest stacked at the edges. See **Spines** below. |
| pane | Not a door word. It means Herdr's or tmux's compositor box. The door's code still names a tile's class `Pane` (`ReaderPane`, `PaneView`, …), a rename left for later; `pane.split` is the one `pane.*` action left, and `tile=` names a tile in `act`. |

### Spines

A row or flow keeps its open tiles together and stacks the rest as spines (PIE-1208, PIE-1223). The container's
`spines` policy says where a pushed-out tile goes; `min-width` says when opening one folds another.

| What happens | Where the spine goes |
|---|---|
| An open pushes a tile out of the open group (`spines: book`, the default) | Its own side's stack, newest nearest the open tiles; it never crosses the open tiles (a page slides, it doesn't flip). |
| You fold a tile by hand (click its fold, `^W` fold) | Where it is, even between two open tiles: nothing moves. |
| An open whose note already has a tile here | that tile, unfolded; never a copy |
| You open a spine | It joins the open group with an even share. Below `min-width` (40 columns by default; 0 never folds) the open tile touched longest ago folds instead. |
| Align left / centre / right (the container's menu, `^W`, `layout.gather side=left\|centre\|right`) | Every spine to one edge, or split evenly around the open tiles (an odd one to the right). It sticks as the `spines` policy until changed. |

```text
 align left:   open pair at the left, every spine stacked on the right
 ┌────────────┐┌────────────┐
 │     A      ││     B      │ |||||||||||||| (14)
 └────────────┘└────────────┘

 align centre: the pair in the middle, spines split evenly (7 | 7)
         ┌────────────┐┌────────────┐
 ||||||| │     A      ││     B      │ |||||||
         └────────────┘└────────────┘

 align right:  every spine stacked on the left, open pair at the right
                     ┌────────────┐┌────────────┐
 (14) |||||||||||||| │     A      ││     B      │
                     └────────────┘└────────────┘
```

The reuse rule applies to automatic opens. Explicit split and `tile.open` readers, pinned readers and wired
destinations stay independent; a fixed slot is never removed by a history landing.

Which side and how many is `placement()`'s spine rule (`src/desk/screen-layout.ts`), the one owner; the showcase's
book-spines section runs all of it.

### View and tile

| Canonical | Meaning | Code | Tree/Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| view | What a screen arranges: board, desk, river, BBS list | each `Screen` | Tree / Detail |
| tile | A framed rectangle that shows one thing and takes focus: a kind from the registry (outline, reader, preview, terminal, board, river, brief, an extension's, …) with a name, dragged by its header (Layout, above) | the class `Pane` in the desk's tree (`pan:28`), `TileKind` `src/desk/tile-kinds.ts`, `makeTile` `src/desk/tiles.ts` | Herdr pane |
| — alias: region | Gone (PIE-511): the board's areas are tiles with names (`preview`, `detail1`, `tree`, a lane's) | — | *collides:* PreviewRegion |
| — alias: column | A river strip; stacked tiles inside. On the engine: a column of a `flow` (PIE-513), a tile or tiles stacked in it | `Col` `riv:69` `PaneS` `riv:28`; `Flow` `lay`, `flw` | none |
| layout tree | Containers (splits by weight, tab sets, docks, columns, flows) over tile ids, each with its policy; floats above it; changed only by the screen-layout module's operations | `LNode` `lay`, `LayoutState` `sl` (the desk and the screens built on it, the board one of them; the river next, as a flow) | none |
| tab set | Tiles stacked in one place, one shown; the header shows the tabs | `Tabs` `lay` | none |
| header | A tile's top border: its number and name (or its tabs), what it shows, `→ link`, `⇤ docked` (the arrow is its edge: `⇤ ⇥ ⤒ ⤓`; a click undocks it). Its title or tabs are the grip: drag them to move the tile. The bare line after them is the border above, so dragging it resizes the tile (a header with no border above is all grip); with alt or ctrl held, the whole header is that border, so a drag on a cramped title grows or shrinks the tile itself | `header` `dsk` | pane title |
| drop | Where a dragged tile lands: tabs (a header or a centre), a split (a triangle), an outer edge; outlined while dragging (the ghost) | `dropAt` `src/desk/drop.ts` | none |
| attached surface | Work about the note in front of you without leaving it (a comment, its sources, the edit preview, the element panel, tune for this note): one part with one set of placements (inline, floating within the tile's borders, split, popup, its own tile), one placement menu, the same keys and way back. ADR 0007 | `surface/attached.ts`: shared geometry, frame input and tile lifecycle; the comment box, Sources, list children and edit preview use its placement menu in `NoteSurface` | none |
| glass, post-it, shelf | The glass: the host desk's floats, kept above every screen through `apply`. `tile.float to=glass` (`^W U`) moves any tile there. A post-it is a `ReaderPane` with one bounded `NoteSurface` history: ←/→, header arrows and title wheel use the reader's back/forward, saved across restarts. `postit.peel` (`alt+E`, header peel) pins the shown entry separately, keeping the hover stack; from the drawer it returns that tile to the glass. `postit.drop` (`alt+X`, header or tile menu drop) removes only the shown entry. The ordinary `tile.dock`, `tile.drawer` and drag landings dock or tuck it; there is no separate set-aside list. `glass.shelf name= colour=` makes a named, coloured ref pile: a `ListView` as list/grid (`shelf.layout`, v, header), selecting and previewing on one click, opening on the second. Its colour is `shelf.colour` (c, header), its name `tile.title`. The shared drop zones receive a post-it through `postit.shelve`; `glass.shelve ref= shelf=` and `glass.pin ref= shelf=` add refs without moving the person's post-it or pick. Shelf refs, layout and colour persist with the host layout. ◇ / `alt+u` cycles off, on and sticky; alt-hover keeps one sticky, Esc dismisses, alt-click ◇ / `alt+U` clears. Pins and shelves count toward the soft limit; an agent's clear keeps the tile the person is in. Tune → Glass adjusts delays and limits (ADR 0007). | `src/glass.ts`, `src/desk/post-it.ts`, `src/desk/shelf.ts`, `surface/list-view.ts`, `surface/navigation.ts` (the shared saved trail), `desk/drop.ts` and `TileKind.receive`; host rendering in `src/drawer.ts`; showcase `glass` | none |
| strip | A one-row run of segments with one current (tile tabs, breadcrumbs, tune's rows, the power bar's scopes, status chips, hint rows): one overflow rule (current keeps its name; others shrink; ‹ › and `+N` opens a list) and one set of keys (designed, pie `03cc2e43`; ADR 0007) | planned | none |
| a click in a collection | Only a link in prose navigates on the first click; a row, card or list item in any collection (tables, figures, lists, links tiles) selects and previews on a click, opens on a second click or ⏎, alt-click opens fresh (Evan, Oct 18 and 21; ADR 0007) | `RowView.press`, `NoteSurface` element clicks | Tree rows |
| wire (a tile's) | A separate route for `select`, `open`, `alt` and `hover` on screen-layout state (PIE-1019). Each verb has one destination, replaced when wired again, without chaining; several sources may feed a preview. Groups do not send wires: address the inner tile. Saved `select` wires keep every distinct destination (several readers may follow one pick, PIE-849) and drop true duplicates; saved `open`, `alt` and `hover` wires keep their last destination; saved group wires are dropped. `tile.link verb=select\|open\|alt\|hover to=<tile>` changes a wire through `apply`; open is the default (`alt+l`). Hover waits for the glass's open delay before resolving a target; without a hover wire it lands in the glass post-it (`alt+l v` sets the wire). `tile.preview` (`^W V`) creates a select wire for a list; on a reader it creates an open target. Old saved links become open wires, and a preview's old `source: tile:` becomes a select wire when loaded. `layout.get` and `tile.info` report wires. A tree with no select target moves nothing while browsing: its frame says **nowhere**, and alt+l wires it. There is no current note (PIE-1207). | `wires`, `placement`, `selectTargets` in `src/desk/screen-layout.ts`; showcase `select-open` | linked pane (Herdr alt-l) |
| home, subject (a list's) | A list of notes (the links tile, an inline `::links`, a river column's links) has a **subject**, the note it lists the links of, and a **home**, the reader it belongs to: the reader the links tile was opened beside (`source=tile:<name>`), or the reader an inline block is drawn in. `⏎` replaces the home's note unless a link says otherwise; `alt+⏎` opens a new held reader beside it; only `b` changes the subject (PIE-794) | `BacklinksPane.home`, `.target`; `TileKind.home`, `TileFacts.home`; `placement()` | Detail's panel (always the note shown) |
| opens are local | A reader's own navigation (a link followed in it, `⏎` on a row in it) changes only the tile it lands in: never another reader, and the outline's cursor only for the person's open (placement's `reveal`). A reader no list's select wire feeds keeps its old note as a spine when an open aimed at it (its own link, `tile=`) isn't a wire's; a list's reader, and an open naming no tile, replace. `alt+r` (or a click on its crumbs) shows a reader's note in the outline on demand (`reader.reveal`) | `Desk.landFrom`, `revealInOutline` | the Tree's `r` |
| layout (named) | The tile tree with each tile's spec, links, docks and open rule, saved by name | `LayoutSpec` `src/desk/tiles.ts` | none |
| open rule | Where an open with no link lands: the first reader that isn't pinned (`reader`, the default), the next column of its flow (`next`), or a reader beside it (`beside`). It's policy (PIE-513): a container's `opens`, a flow's `next` when it says none; a layout's `rule` is read as the screen's; a saved `current` reads as the default | `opens` in `Policy` `lay`, `placement` `sl` | none |
| terminal tile | A program in a pty the door owns; the person is *in* it (like entered) until `ctrl+]` | `PtyPane` `src/desk/pty.ts` | Herdr pane |
| focus | Which tile gets the keys | `focus` in each screen | pane focus |
| entered | You are *inside* a reader's edit, comment or panel | `Entered` `pan:287` | draft focus |
| zoom | One tile fills the screen | `tile.zoom` (`^W z`): the desk and every screen on it, the board too (#98) | Herdr zoom |
| float | A tile taken out of the tree to its own rectangle, above everything (`tile.float`, the board's `o`, `^W f`); its title drags it, its ◢ corner sizes it, the frame card's float or put-back row changes where it sits | `Desk.floats`, `ScreenLayout.floats` `lay`, `float.place` | none |
| dock | A container sliding over the others from an edge (PIE-505): on the desk any tile or tab set docked to an edge (`^W p`, `tile.dock`, at an outer edge with `edge=`); it stays on that screen (a tile that travels goes in your drawer), holding anything moved into it (a drop on its handle, said `docked here: stays on this screen`, a move beside a tile in it); shuts when the keys leave (unless `collapsible` or `overlay` is off, or it `stays`); a shut one is a handle at the end of the hint row (`⇤ tree+now`, or a named container's name: the board's `⇤ outline`, `⤓ links`). The board's outline (the tree over its preview, a left dock) and backlinks (the list beside its preview, a bottom dock that stays) are desk docks | `Dock` `lay` | none |
| lock | A layout's shape (the screen's, or your drawer's: each has its own, and says which, PIE-1346) fixed for a task, its contents live: nothing moves, drops, opens as a new tile, closes, resizes, goes in or out of a dock, relinks or loads; reading, editing, terminals, docks sliding, tabs and zoom go on. Saved, so it comes back after a restart | `layout.lock` (no key: the hint row's `□ lock` / `▣ screen locked` / `▣ drawer locked` chips, the drawer frame's chip, the power bar's "lock your drawer" row, `^W P`), `Policy.locked`, the layout's name `Ctx.self` | none |
| agent policy | What an agent may do to a tile (PIE-639): `free`, `edit` (edit its note; no navigating, closing or retargeting it) or `off` (peek only). A screen default and a tile's own exceptions, saved with the layout, a chip on the tile's frame (`✎ agents: edit only`, `⊘ agents: hands off`), enforced for agents only: the layout module's operations and the dispatcher's actor rule share `agentRefusal`. The lock (`layout.lock`) fixes the shape for everyone and leaves contents live; the agent policy limits only agents, tile by tile, and leaves the person free | `Policy.agents`, `LayoutState.agents`, `agentLevel` (`src/desk/screen-layout.ts`), `src/surface/agent-level.ts`, `Desk.agentTile` | none |
| spine | A tile folded to a title strip (`tile.collapse`; the drawer itself as a one-row strip when a lone drawer tile folds, PIE-1254; the board's `c` on a lane or a reader; the frame card's fold row; placement keeps an unwired replaced held note as an inner spine; a wire aimed at a tile replaces in place with Back history, PIE-1206), vertical or horizontal; a click opens it, its `×` closes it without opening it (`tile.close`, as `^W x`); it always names what it holds, a tile with no title yet its number and name ("4 detail"); under Kitty graphics its rotated title, running down under your drawer or a dock, is cut at that edge (`uncover`, src/kitty.ts), never dropped whole | `drawSpine`, `drawHSpine` (`src/spine.ts`), `Desk.collapsed`; river `Cover` `riv:48` | none |
| pick | A tile selected for gathering, without moving focus. `tile.select`, shift+click on its title, or `^W space` toggles it. The person's picks show ◆; an agent's picks are its own and show ◇. `^W G` gathers the picked tiles, even one, in their existing arrangement | `Desk.pickedBy`, `tile.select`, `tile.group selected=true` | none |
| gathering | Moving tiles into a group whole, with their running programs, policy, spine and links. `^W G` gathers picks, or offers the focused tile and its split when nothing is picked. Spilling returns the group's tiles to the containing screen | `tile.group`, `groupSpec`, `Desk.groupTiles` | none |
| link across an edge | A tile's link to a tile on another desk, inside a group (`<group>/<tile>`) or outside its own group (`../<tile>`). It keeps the target and role through gathering, moving, spilling and saving. The source tile's lock policy applies to creation, replacement, role changes and removal. Link mode belongs to the top desk: Alt+L, then a click, screen-wide number or h j k l reaches across groups and the drawer; the stored tile id keeps the wire; paths are input and display | `ExtLink`, `Desk.linkTile`, `Desk.pathTo` | none |
| mount | A screen spec drawn live in a tile of another screen (PIE-651): the whole screen (`screen=board`), a part of it (`part=lanes`, the container of that key) or a **group** (tiles gathered from the screen into one, `tile.group`: how a tab holds a split). Its own desk inside the tile, its own layout and selection, saved in the tile's spec; folds to a spine named for the screen; its frame/menu pops it out to its full screen or enters it, `q` comes back. Alt and ^W act on its focused tile; 1-9 and Tab stay with the holder. Drawn as a header over its tiles' own frames, with no hint row of its own (the screen's one row speaks for the focused tile); named for its screen | `ScreenTile` (`src/desk/screen-tile.ts`), `mountDesk`, `partSpec`, `groupSpec` | none |
| lane | A saved view shown as a board column: a query tile in the board's columns | `QueryPane` (`src/desk/query.ts`) | virtual branch |
| card | One block in a lane | lane items | branch root row |

### Reading and editing (the note surface)

| Canonical | Meaning | Code | Tree/Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| Blockdown | The source language a note is written in: Markdown-adjacent (prose, lists, fences, links) plus the outline's own forms (`[key::value]` properties, `((id))` references and embeds, `[[page]]` links, `::component` blocks, heading and callout styles). Not a Markdown dialect with compatibility promises: it may deviate wherever the outline needs to. Markdown is one render target (export, the publisher, MCP's `resources/read`), with HTML and terminal drawing beside it | outline-core's grammars (`header-line.ts`, `link-syntax.ts`, `code-fence.ts`, the property grammar) | the note's text |
| code is opaque | What a code span or fenced code holds is text (PIE-764): a `((ref))`, `[[page]]`, `!((embed))`, `[key::value]`, `#tag` or completion trigger in one is never a link, a property, a backlink, a reference warning or a popup. One rule, outline-core's: a code span is a backtick run closed by the next run of exactly as many on the **same line** (Blockdown: a span never crosses a line, so a backtick can't hide the next line's properties), and an **unclosed run is text** (CommonMark: it hides nothing). Fences (in list items and quotes too) and indented code hide links; a component's YAML and `<!-- literal -->` regions hide properties. The link scans leave code out by default; a line-by-line reader passes `"inline"`. Completion is the one exception for intent: after an unclosed backtick before the cursor the person is typing a span, so no popup opens | `codeSpanRanges`, `codeHides`, `cursorInCode`, `protectedCodeRanges`, `scanPropertyLiteralRanges` (outline-core `src/code-ranges.ts`); `CodeScope` (`src/link-syntax.ts`) | the same scans |
| partial-safe | A construct left open costs only itself (PIE-669): an unclosed fence ends at its **paragraph** (the first blank line or fence opener after it; Blockdown deviates from CommonMark, which runs it to the end), and a line past its first paragraph opening another fence like it with an info string means it was never closed (so it doesn't pair with a later code block's closing line and shift every fence after it); an unclosed `[[`, `((` or `[key::` is text on its own line, and never swallows the next link or property on it; an unclosed `::component` or `<!-- literal -->` is plain text; a `::graph-*` whose body is only `key: value` lines, with no `---` lines, is read as Markdown rows, so its props never arrive, and the figure says so wherever it is drawn or listed (the door's figure and ⚠ rows, Detail's line after it, the published page's figure; `propsWithoutRules`, PIE-941). Each one is **said**: in the reader above the note (⚠ rows), after a save (the flash, `ep0ch new`), and in an agent tool's `warnings`, with how to close it. Detail and the publisher close an open fence where the service ends it | `codeBlocks`, `fenceEnd` (outline-core `src/code-fence.ts`), `closeOpenFences` (`src/component-block.ts`), `syntaxWarnings` (`src/syntax-warnings.ts`) | the same scans; Detail's presentation |
| reader | Any tile or screen hosting a note surface. The `reader` tile keeps what's opened or wired into it; **▣ pinned**, it keeps its note (or a page's, shown again at startup) and every open aimed at it lands in the reader beside it, its hint row naming where. `p`, the pin chip and `reader.pin [on=] [page=]` pin it; `P` opens the policy panel. `reader.follow source=<tile>` sets a select wire into it; sources pick those targets on screen with `alt+l s`, through `tile.link verb=select`, never a separate follow wiring | `ReaderPane` (`pinned`, `setPin`), `MessageReader` `scr:273` | Detail |
| note surface | Draws, folds, edits, comments on a note | `NoteSurface` `note:107` | Detail body |
| mode (a reader's) | What takes a reader's keys besides reading: a step's status choice, the property panel, the edit, the comment session; open ones form its mode stack, in a fixed precedence | `ReaderMode`, `ModeStack`, `PRECEDENCE` (`src/surface/modes.ts`) | edit mode, inspector |
| surface host | What a view gives it: ctx, redraw, navigate, and maybe its own header rows | `SurfaceHost` `note:43` | none |
| look | The spacing, list density and surfaces something is drawn with (PIE-673, PIE-675): measure, padding, margin, list gap, dividers, zebra, heading spacing, breakpoints, a surface (`bg`), a frame (`border`), an edge in a tone, the header's surface and picture; resolved from the cascade's levels for where it's drawn, each value with where it came from. Drawn, never text | `Look`, `lookFor` (`src/look.ts`), outline-core `resolveStyle` | none yet (slice 3) |
| this tile, this list | The cascade's two nearest levels (PIE-675): one tile's own look, kept in its tile spec (`look`, saved with the layout); one list's own, its `[style.list.…]` tokens on its first item (PIE-1135), over defaults on its section heading or lead-in. Nearest wins: built-in, global, tile kind, screen, page, this tile, this list or a box | outline-core `listLayers`, `listOwners`, `listStart`; `Pane.instanceLook`, `Desk.tileLooks`; `NoteSurface.listAt`; the reader's lists and the links tile through `list-look.ts` | showcase `style-one-list` |
| surface | A background the look draws (PIE-675): a theme role (`raised`, `sunken`) or a tone (the callouts' families) at a strength, never a colour; dark-capped in every theme. A tile's sits under its content and yields to a picture drawn under the text; a box's inside its frame | `surfaceMix`, `surfaceRgb` (`src/theme.ts`), `surfaceBg` (`src/style.ts`), `Canvas.under`, `Doc.tints` | none yet |
| style note | A note (or any line of one) that sets look values for a level: `[style-for::global\|tile:<kind>\|screen:<name>\|<a name>]` with `[style.<token>::value]` fields; a page uses a named one with `[style::name]` | outline-core `styleSheetsFromBlocks`, the service's `styles.list` | shown as written |
| tune inspector | The tile under a tile that shows its look's values and sources and nudges them live (in memory: the tuning), saving to the level picked; beside it when too short to split | `TunePane`, `TUNE_ACTIONS` (`src/desk/tune.ts`), `Tuning` (`src/look.ts`) | none |
| content rect | Where a tile's content is drawn inside its frame: less its padding, a note's held to its measure and centred; the mouse is mapped through it | `contentRect` (`src/desk/desk.ts`) | none |
| preview | The reader that follows the selection; on the desk, a preview tile follows a source (a tile, a file) | board `preview`, `PreviewPane` | **Preview** |
| detail | A word for a reader in held mode (opened on purpose with `⏎` or `alt+⏎`, or started held: `^W o d`), not a tile kind of its own since PIE-705. `kind: detail` in a saved layout or a screen spec reads as a `reader` with `mode: held` (`mode: pinned` when it has `page=`) | `canonSpec` (`src/desk/tiles.ts`), `canonKind` (`src/desk/layout.ts`) | **Current** |
| header | Title, crumbs, summary line, notices | `render` `note:268` | menu row + title |
| summary line | Chosen properties under the title | `summary` `note:366` | summary keys |
| property panel | Every property token, editable | `PropertyPanel` `pp:85` | property inspector |
| settings card | A configurable note's fields from its component schema, each set with a picker in the power bar (`,`) | `SettingsCard` (`src/surface/settings-card.ts`), the property panel's other face | config form, inspector |
| edit control | Frame + status + keys for any draft | `renderEditor` `ed:25` | Detail edit mode |
| draft | Unsaved text with its base revision | `Draft` (`src/edit.ts`) | draft |
| draft session | One draft's lifecycle: open (restoring), type, write or leave, put aside, the hold, the agent rule | `DraftSession` (`src/draft-session.ts`) | none |
| unsent | A draft put aside (esc twice, a closed screen, quitting) where it was written, with a copy on disk. Its line under the reader's header says what the **three-way comparison** found (the edit's own changes against the note now: already in the note, still new, changed differently since) in one calm sentence and offers `[show them]`/`[compare]`, `[add them]`, `[keep as a note]` and `[let it go]`; an edit the note already has settles quietly (a copy kept), an old one on an older revision folds to a `1 old edit` chip. An edit opened by mistake (a few stray characters, briefly) closes on one esc and isn't put aside: ctrl+z brings the strays back | `unsent.*` actions; `src/unsent-compare.ts`, `src/unsent.ts`, `src/stray.ts`, `shelve` in `src/draft-session.ts` | none |
| target adapter | Where a draft session writes: a block's text, a comment or reply, a new card or child | `blockTarget`, `commentTarget`, `cardTarget` (`src/draft-session.ts`) | save, comment, capture |
| unsent (put aside) | A draft closed with its text (esc twice, a screen closed, the door quitting, a refused click away): kept where it was written, copied to `drafts/`, brought back by opening the same draft | `shelve`, `unsent`, `DraftSession.open` (`src/draft-session.ts`) | none |
| invitation | The person's `@name` line in their own draft: that agent may reply once with new text for the lines above it (a seam; not wired to an action yet) | `DraftSession.invite`, `reply` (`src/draft-session.ts`) | none |
| composer | Board draft for a new card or note: a draft session with the card adapter, drawn over the board | `Composer` `del` | Quick Capture (close) |
| comment session | Passage picker, comment, threads | `CommentSession` | passage comments |
| completion | `[[`, `((`, `[file::` popup | `cmp`; wired at `note:592` | completion |
| fold | Folded heading, list item or callout (reader state) | `note:835` | Folding |
| selection | Text selected in a reader (reading state). One made with the mouse is copied when the button comes up (copy on select, as Herdr's `ui.copy_on_select`; `EP0CH_COPY_ON_SELECT=0` turns it off); `y`, `Y`, cmd+c or the copy control copy it any time. An agent's is never copied to the person's clipboard | `Selection` `sel:52` | text selection |
| element | What `[ ]` stops on in a reader: a link, a fold, a figure row, an embed, a resource projection, a comment mark, a checklist step; one is current | `elements` `note` | none |
| reading ruler | The tint under the block the current element is in | `RULER_BG` `sel` | none |
| item focus | The row the cursor is on inside the tile, figure or table that has the keys (PIE-969): a band and a left-edge bar over the container's ruler tint; every list's selected row is the same band. A theme value (`theme.focus.item`, with `focus.container` and `focus.selection` beside it) | `selectedRow()` `style.ts`, `ITEM_BG` `selection.ts` | none |
| tint | A block (maybe a passage) tinted in the ruler's colour inside one reader, with who set it named; PIE-423's "focus mark". `block.tint` | `block.tint` `note` | focus mark (PIE-423) |
| attention mark | A mark on a block (or an nvim line) with a reason and who set it, framed and labelled in every tile showing it, until dismissed | `block.mark` `src/desk/marks.ts` | focus mark (PIE-423's shared part) |
| embed | `!((id))` transclusion region; `!((id^fragment))` shows the fragment's slice; nested to the service's depth. Drawn quietly: a dim, clickable `» note` source line (`≡ view` for a view) and a dim bar; only a problem heading stays loud | `src/embeds.ts` | generated embed |
| step | A checklist item (`- [ ]`, `[x]`, `[~]`, `[!]`, `[✓]` approved, `[✗]` rejected: outline-core `checklist-marks.ts`) whose box is a control, in the note or an embed | `task` elements, `src/steps.ts` | checklist control (PIE-367, PIE-811) |

### Agents and presence

| Canonical | Meaning | Code | Tree/Detail |
|---|---|---|---|

| action set | Actions for one host kind: note, board, desk, river | `ActionSet` `act:37` | none |

| repaints | Whether a run changed what's shown: left out, an action touching `nothing` changed nothing and every other did; `true`, `false` or a function of its answer. The person's press repaints once for it, never twice | `ActionDef.repaints`, `asPerson` | none |
| whereabouts | Where the person is: the screen, the tile with their focus, the tile they type in, busy, idle, away, and who takes their next key (the door's own layer over everything, the drawer or the screen, a program under them: PIE-1438); the shell's one answer | `App.person()`, `src/whereabouts.ts` | none |
