| activity | Recent edits by user, agent, system | `scr:392`, `pan:375` | none |

### Words that mean two things

| Word | Meaning A | Meaning B | Use instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reader | BBS screen `Reader` (now `MessageReader` `scr:273`, a surface host: resolved by PIE-426) | `ReaderPane` `pan:184` | "reader" = any surface host |
| pin | A reader keeps its note; opens land beside it (`reader.pin`, PIE-1207) | River column resists squeeze `riv:879` | a tile is **docked** to an edge or **undocked** into the layout (`tile.dock`: `^W p` toggles, `T`, `B`, a click on a header's `⇤ docked` undocks); `p` **pins** a reader (`p pin`, `reader.pin`) and **holds** elsewhere: a river column holds its full width (`p hold full`, `tile.hold`), a `#tag`'s list holds its tag off its reader (`p hold`, `tile.hold`, PIE-855) |
| dock, drawer | Your drawer: the host layer's, it travels across screens | A screen's edge container a tile is docked into (PIE-505; a "drawer" until the words were swapped); before PIE-498, a river column resisting squeeze (`tile.dock`) | **drawer** = your drawer only, the host layer's (`tile.drawer`); **dock** = a screen's edge container and docking a tile to an edge (`tile.dock`); a float goes **back** into the layout; a river column is **held** (`tile.hold`) |
| preview | Board reader following the card | Desk `/` search's right half `dsk:404` | A only |
| detail | Door: a reader you opened (*Current*) | Outliner: the Detail pane | owner decides |
| region | Board pane (gone with PIE-511: the board's areas are named tiles) | Outliner PreviewRegion (focusable item) | "tile" |
| spine | River compression tier | Board collapsed lane or reader | fine: same idea, one part (`src/spine.ts`) |
| `alt+⏎` | Board: second detail | Outliner: keep Preview as Current | a link's `alt+⏎` (or a ctrl- or alt-click) opens beside, never in a tile's link (PIE-473) |
| focus | The person's keys: which tile has them (`tile.focus`, `focus`) | PIE-423's "focus mark" | **focus** is only the keys; an in-reader highlight is a **tint** (`block.tint`); a mark with a reason is an **attention mark** (`block.mark`) |

---

## 2. The grammar tree

The proposed tree, refined against what the code does. `[x]` = exists in shared code today,
`[~]` = exists but per screen, `[ ]` = missing.

```
shell                                     App (app:72)
├── [x] screen switcher                   MainMenu + stack; screen.open / screen.back on every screen (PIE-489)
├── [x] drop to shell                     screen.shell: the menu's !, the desk's ^W ! (src/drop.ts, App.suspend)
├── [ ] workspace / outline switcher      one SocketBoard per run (PIE-427)
├── [x] status bar, flash                 app:242, app:116
├── [x] the drawer (PIE-498)              src/drawer.ts: a chip on the status bar, your tabs over any screen; any tile goes in (tile.drawer, ^W a)
├── [~] hint row                          every screen draws its own
├── [x] unsaved guard                     app:108 (asks twice, copies drafts)
├── [x] agent entry: peek snap open act   control.ts, app:151-194
└── view                                  a Screen
    ├── [x] header                        note surface; BBS rows via SurfaceHost.header
    ├── [~] primary content               lanes | layout tree | strip | list
    ├── [x] detail = reader(s)            NoteSurface in every view
    └── [~] hint row
tile operations
    ├── [~] focus (Tab, number, click)    every view, 3 implementations
    ├── [~] zoom                          desk and the screens on it, the board too (#98); tile.zoom; not the river (PIE-428)
    ├── [~] open beside / split           desk ^W o, board alt+⏎, river ⏎/s; tile.open, pane.split
    ├── [~] close                         desk ^W x, board x, river x; tile.close (desk, board)
    ├── [~] resize                        desk drag/^W<>, board drag/^W<>: one tree; tile.resize
    ├── [~] move                          desk: drag a header, ^W m t T H J K L (layout.move, tabs on drop); board float HJKL; tile.float, tile.dock; into the drawer: tile.drawer, ^W a
    ├── [~] squeeze (width tiers)         river full/peek/spine around the wide column (w widens); board lanes, readers c · one spine part
    └── [~] persist layout                the tree to desk.json, delivery.json (same fields); river.json
entity navigation
    ├── [~] children                      links tile's Children group, river replies, BBS T
    ├── [x] up                            note surface u
    ├── [x] outlinks (in-note links)      note surface [ ] ⏎ click; the tree's L lists a row's
    ├── [~] backlinks                     board dock, backlinks tile, the tree's L (Detail's view, PIE-442)
    ├── [~] resources list                the tree's L: a row's resources, ⏎ shows one (PIE-432)
    ├── [~] search / jump                 4 versions
    └── [~] history (back/forward)        note surface (board, desk); river (between columns, SurfaceHost.history); BBS next (PIE-453)
extensions
    ├── [x] properties (summary, panel)   note surface
    ├── [x] edit, comments, completion    note surface + ed
    ├── [x] folds                         note surface (not river cards)
    ├── [~] presence, activity, stats     BBS screens + desk tiles (PIE-430)
    └── [~] view actions                  board cards, river columns
```

Refinements to the proposed tree:

- **"Entered" belongs in tile operations.** Focus picks the tile; *entered* says whether keys go
  into the reader's edit/comment/panel. It's shared (`Entered` `pan:287`) and it's how agents
  never steal keys.
- **The hint row is part of the view.** Every view has one; only the edit control's is shared
  (`editHint` `ed:48`).
- **Squeeze is a tile operation,** not a river feature. The board already has a manual version.
- **History was missing** from the proposed tree and from the code; the outliner's Detail has it. Readers
  have it since PIE-453 (back and forward, alt+← alt+→, the side buttons).

## 4. Findings

### F1. Two readers, two body renderers

- `NoteSurface` renders with `renderDoc` (`note:320`). The desk search preview (`dsk:404`) and river
  cards (`riv:362`) use `wrap` + `colourBody`.
- **Resolved for the BBS by PIE-426.** The BBS message reader (`MessageReader` `scr:273`) hosts
  `NoteSurface` through `SurfaceHost`, like the board, desk and river: links you step to and open, the
  property panel, folds, comments, edit, selection and copy, the ruler, resource projections, and `act`.
  Its header stays the BBS one (`Date`, `To` from `to::`, `From`, `Reply`, `Subj`, `Conf`, `Stat`),
  given to the surface through `SurfaceHost.header`, which takes the place of the surface's title, byline
  and crumbs (the summary line follows it). Its old body code is gone. Its own keys are actions
  (`message.next`, `message.previous`, `message.thread`); where they meet the surface's, the surface
  goes first (key audit below, under F4). A followed link opens as the next message reader on the screen
  stack; an agent's never opens over an edit, comment or panel the person is in.
- River cards stay a list. A full river column's note body is drawn by the surface (`NoteSurface.digest`,
  PIE-472: the same renderer, transclusions and step controls, inside the river's own column and scroll);
  its header and replies are still the river's, and PIE-431 decides the rest (folds, comment marks).

### F2. Pane operations are re-implemented per view

- Desk: layout tree (`lay:5`), zoom, `^W` prefix. Board: fractions (`del:37`), floats, docks,
  `x` and `o` without a prefix. River: strip, automatic squeeze, `x`, `p`, `s`.
- Zoom was the desk's only (it swaps the rectangle map, `dsk:177`, not a tree op); the board has it since it became a preset on the desk (#98). The river still has none.
- **Resolved for the board by PIE-511:** it is a screen on the desk (a spec since PIE-515), so its floats, docks, zoom and `^W` keys are
  the desk's; its `x` and `o` are the same tile actions without a prefix.
- **Resolved for the river by PIE-515:** its columns are a flow of tiles on the desk: its `w` `p` `x` `s` and back and
  forward are the engine's tile actions (`tile.widen`, `tile.hold`, `tile.close`, `column.split`, `tile.travel`), and zoom
  and `^W` work there as on any desk.
- **Resolves:** PIE-412 (one layout tree), then PIE-428 (zoom everywhere) on top of it, PIE-413
  (tabs, drag-and-drop).
- **PIE-412 slice 1 (done):** the board is on the desk's tree. Its lanes pane (columns are drawn inside
  it), preview and details (the `readers` row), backlinks dock (under the readers) and outline dock
  (beside everything) are leaves, the docks sliding over (`over`) until undocked, floats above. The keys,
  hints and `delivery.json` fields are unchanged. Slice 2 moves the river's strip onto it; slice 3 stores
  the tree in the outline as screen notes.

### F3. Agents can read and edit, but not arrange (resolved by PIE-506)

- Since PIE-514 every screen's actions run through one dispatcher per screen host, and each declares what it
  touches: the screen-local `act`s, tile pickers and person checks are gone.

- Every reader shares `NOTE_ACTIONS` (`note:1329`), and since PIE-506 every key and click the note surface
  handles runs one of them (`scroll`, `element.select by=`, `fold.select`, `callouts`, `select.mode`, …).
- Desk: `open`, `tile.*`, `layout.*` by stable id with a layout revision (PIE-412, PIE-413,
  PIE-491), the policy's `layout.lock` and `layout.policy` (PIE-505), and since PIE-506 `search`, `tile.enter` (the terminal kind's),
  `tile.leave` and each tile kind's own (`activity.pick`, `tree.fold`, `backlinks.fold`, `backlinks.groups`,
  `reader.pin`, registered on the reader kind).
- Board: the desk's `tile.*`, `card.*`, and since PIE-506 `board.hub` (the `g` picker), `lane.collapse`,
  `outline`, `float.place`, `list.pick`, `board.reload`; the lane cursor is `card.select by=` (an agent's
  is its own selection).
- River: since PIE-515 a column kind's (`column.select`, `column.replies`, `column.scroll`, `column.filter`, `column.tag`,
  `column.split`, `column.copy`) and the desk's tile actions (`tile.widen`, `tile.hold`, `tile.close`, `tile.travel`); `jump` is gone (`search`).
- BBS: `screen.*`, `list.*`, `message.*`, and since PIE-506 `menu.select`, `list.thread`, `who.refresh`,
  `art.*`, `screen.help` and `video.cycle`.
- **Kept so by test:** `test/parity.ts` (run by `test/parity-*.test.ts`) presses every key and clicks across every screen and fails on a
  change no action declared ([the agent interface](AGENT-INTERFACE.md#parity-every-key-and-click-is-an-action-pie-506)).
- Still direct: dragging a border or a lane edge (a share, not steps; agents resize by `tile.resize` and
  `layout.resize`), moving a keyboard selection after `v`, drag-selecting text, and the desk's `^W r` / `^W w`
  pickers (input states that end in `layout.load` and `screen.save`).

### F4. The same key means different things

| Key | Board | Desk | River | BBS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `c` | collapse a lane or a reader | — (says `C` comments) | — (says so) | — (says so) |
| `C` | comment (was: reopen all lanes; now `alt+c`) | comment | comment | comment |
| `s` | steps | `^W s` swap | split | — |
| `m` | move card / threads in a reader | threads | threads | threads |
| `x` | close detail or float (on the preview: says why it stays) | `^W x` close | close tile | — |
| `o` | pop out float | `^W o` add a tile | — | — |
| `t` | outline dock | `^W o t` add outline | — | thread (`t` `T`) |
| `p` | — | hold reader | hold column (full) | previous (`p` `P`) |
| `w` | — (the backlinks dock's `w` is stage) | — (`^W w` saves the screen) | widen: the focused column takes the wide place | — |
| `f` | fold | fold | filter | fold |
| `alt+⏎` | second detail | — | duplicate column | the next reader, as ⏎ |
| `q` | back (PIE-489) | menu | back (the quote flash moved to `"`) | back |
| `space` | lanes: — · a reader: page (a current step: done / to do) | page (a current step: done / to do) | toggle replies (a column's current step: done / to do) | page (a current step: done / to do) |
| `ctrl+z` | undo a step, callout or image change, an extension action or a quote | undo a step, callout or image change, an extension action or a quote | undo a step, callout or image change, an extension action or a quote | undo a step, callout or image change, an extension action or a quote |

- Since PIE-514 every key on every screen runs its action through the screen's dispatcher (`press`), so one
  key is one action with one rule for agents wherever it's bound; the clashes above are only in which key
  names which action.
- Pane operations should get one key layer (the desk's `^W` is the most complete). Reader keys
  are already consistent because they come from the surface.
- The backlinks dock (PIE-442) takes Detail's backlinks keys where they're free with the dock
  focused: `/` filter (the desk's and river's `/` also search), `s` sort (the lanes' `s` is steps),
  `h` resolved (the lanes' and river's `h` is left; the dock has no left), `n` this note (the lanes'
  `n` is a new card), `.` or space a group. Two clash and are moved: Detail's `k` kind is up in every
  door list, so kind is `K` (a float's `K` nudges it up, only with the float focused); Detail's `t`
  stage is the board's outline dock from any focus, so stage is `w` (the steps overlay's `w`
  "waiting" can't be open at the same time). A filter being typed holds every key, `t b g` included.
- `c` collapses wherever something collapses, and `C` comments in every reader (PIE-440).
- **Steps (PIE-472)** take no new key while reading. `⏎` or a click (or right-click: a step's box keeps its right-click from the tile menu) on a step's box opens
  its status choice, as on any element. Inside the choice `x o + - w !` mark done, to do, approved, rejected,
  waiting and problem (the board's steps overlay uses `x + - w !`; `+` and `-` are the box marks a keyboard types for
  approved and rejected, PIE-811), `y` copies the step link (`y` copies everywhere) and
  `a` makes it addressable; the choice takes every key first on the board, the desk, the river and the
  BBS reader until a choice or `esc`, so their `x` (close), `o` (pop out, add tile) and `w` (the
  backlinks dock's stage) can't fire. Outside it, `space` toggles a step only while the step is the
  current element in view (as Detail's space on a focused checkbox); it pages the reader otherwise, and
  in the river it only reaches the step through the column's `[ ]`. `ctrl+z` undoes the last step change
  you made in this reader (Detail's binding); in a draft it takes back an agent's patch (PIE-501), the same
  word, and nothing else in the door binds it.
- **List children preview (PIE-1139)** uses the `p` fold chip or reader menu (`list.preview`), with no keyboard binding. Within a preview list j/k and a click select, Enter or a second click unfolds in place. Clicking the attachment's placement chip uses the shared menu; clicking its text edits its source lines.

- **Callouts (PIE-538)** take no new key either. A callout is a fold point, so `( )`, `f`, `⏎` on its `▾`/`▸` and a
  click on its title fold it as they fold a heading; `z` (unchanged key) opens every callout, then puts them back as
  written. Its icon is an element: `⏎` or a click opens the type choice, the step choice's picker with `j k ⏎`, and its
  own `-` and `+` (make it start folded or open; nothing else in a choice binds them). `ctrl+z` undoes whichever change
  you made last in this reader, a step's or a callout's. In an edit, `> [!` opens the same completion popup `[[` does.
- **Images (PIE-532)** bind keys only while an image is the `[ ]` position (its caption, or a click on the image): `+`
  and `-` step its width (a quarter, a third, a half, two thirds, three quarters, full; a header's height by two rows),
  `←` `→` move it left, center, right, `H` makes it the note's header or not, and on the header `=` shows it whole or
  cropped to fill (`image.fit`; the reader's `=` is a figure's density only while a figure is current). No key
  brightens an image (`image.dim` is an agent's and the line's). Nothing else in a reader binds `+`
  `-` `H`; the desk's `+` `-` are under `^W` (tile.resize). `←` `→` are taken the way a live figure's are (the host
  asks the reader first, `claims`), so the river's column steps and the BBS reader's previous and next wait until the
  `[ ]` position leaves the image. `H` isn't claimed: in a floating tile it stays `float.place` (the board's lanes' `H`
  moves a card only with the lanes focused), and the caption's `[▀]` makes it the header there. The caption's
  `[−][+] [◂][▸] [▀]` are the same actions by mouse. `ctrl+z` undoes whichever change you made last here.
- **The BBS message reader (PIE-426)** meets the surface's keys this way. An edit, a comment or the
  property panel takes every key (`q`, `n`, `esc` included) until it closes. Otherwise the surface goes
  first and the BBS keys take what it leaves:
  - no clash: `n` `N` `→` next, `p` `P` `←` previous, `t` `T` thread, `q` `Q` back (the surface binds none
    of them while reading); `j k ↑↓` move the reading line by block; `PgUp PgDn space` scroll;
  - `⏎` acts on the current element when there is one in view (a link opens as the next reader, a fold
    toggles, a comment mark expands its thread); with none it is next, as before. `esc` lets go of the
    element, the selection or a focus mark first; then it is back;
  - `U` is the surface's `u` (up), one code path; at the top it still says so;
  - new to the BBS reader, no old meaning: `[ ] ( ) f F z i I C c m e ctrl+e v y Y`, clicks, drags and the
    wheel; `V` (video) stays the menu's.

### F5. Entity navigation is scattered

- Backlinks: board dock only. Children: three shapes (thread tile, river replies, BBS `T`; since PIE-693 the thread tile is the links tile's Children group). Since
  PIE-442 the dock groups, filters and sorts as Detail does through `src/backlinks.ts`; the PIE-432
  connections screen should list backlinks through it too, not a new list.
- Outlinks are in-note only (`[ ]`); no list of them. No resources list. No history anywhere. (Since then: the
  tree's `L` lists a row's outlinks, resources and backlinks, as the outliner's Tree does; the backlinks tile,
  `^W o l`, shows another tile's; readers have back and forward, PIE-453.)
- **Resolves:** PIE-432 (connections screen: children, outlinks, backlinks, resources) as one
  shared entity-nav component; PIE-413 puts backlinks under details.

### F6. Four searches

| Where | Code | Backend |
|---|---|---|
| BBS `Search` screen | removed (PIE-510) | was `board.search`, reachable from no menu |
| Desk `/` overlay | moved into the power bar's notes scope (PIE-656): `/` opens the bar there, its preview a reader's note surface | `board.search`, debounced, Jev after a pause |
| River `/` jump | removed with `river.ts` (PIE-515) | was a local `tree.index`; the river's `g` is the desk's search now, and its `/` filters a column |
| Board `g` | `del` picker | board (hub) discovery, not text |

- One jump palette, as a shell overlay, serves every view: the power bar (PIE-656). PIE-427's outline switcher can
  be one of its sources.

### F7. Presence and stats are duplicated and BBS-flavoured

- Who's Online and Last Callers: resolved in the demolition pass. `WhoPane` and `ActivityPane` are the one copy each;
  the menu's W and L open them as screen specs (`who`, `lastcall`).
- Stats: one screen (`scr:535`), a heatmap of `updatedAt`, not a tile.
- **Resolves:** PIE-430 (active people and agents, one component used as screen or tile).

### F8. The shell is one workspace for the whole run

- `ctx.board` is fixed at startup (`src/main.ts:38`). Live figures (`src/live.ts:26`) and
  reference caches (`src/refs.ts:25`) are module-level, not per workspace.
- **Resolves:** PIE-427 needs those caches keyed by connection before a switcher is safe.

### F9. The "one edit control" is split in two

- `ed` owns the frame, status line, hint and `$EDITOR` handoff.
- Completion is wired only inside `NoteSurface` (`note:592`) and `CommentSession`
  (`src/comment.ts:243`). The board's composer uses `renderEditor` (`del:1335`) but gets no
  completion.
- **Resolves:** PIE-434 (see §5: the editing component should own completion).
- **Status (PIE-516):** every draft is a draft session, and its `key` takes the host's completer; the
  board's composer passes one, so it completes `[[` `((` `[file::` as the reader's edit and comment do.

### F10. Stale words in the code

- Help said "Read-only. Nothing you do here writes to the outline." It writes now; the line is gone.
- `note:2` says the surface is hosted "(next) a river column". The river hosts it already.
- Not fixed here: the audit changed no behaviour or strings.

## 5. Core versus extensions (PIE-434)

### Collections select, preview and open (PIE-849)

A saved VIEW record or `::view{…} ^name` uses the same `ListView` over `RowView` in every arrangement.
The service supplies the rows and groups. A click picks a card and gives the list the keys; `j k` step rows,
`h l` cross gallery columns or board lanes, and `H L` cross groups. These bindings are declared in
`surface/list-keys.ts` and dispatch `list.*`, as clicks and `act` do. Card edges use `BORDER_BOXES`; the pick
has a bright, bold frame and title, with no cell background. Small and large keep their cover sizes.

`landing()` applies the record's `open` after a tile's wire and its container's `opensInto`. `open=float`
creates a reader tile with a select wire and `peekOf` ownership on its float: subsequent picks reuse only
the view's own peek while the view keeps the keys. Enter enters it; Esc in the gallery or the peek closes it.
Replacing the source note or closing its tile retires the owned peek and wire through the existing close rules,
as the actor who changed the source. A refused close releases the peek into an ordinary float: an agent never
takes its reader's keys or editor. A person's floated reader stays, and its select wire still delivers picks.
The peek's
`⤢` calls `tile.float on=false where=right`, landing it beside its source and releasing the select wire.
An explicit landing refuses a container that takes no drops; plain `tile.float on=false` puts it back
where it was opened and keeps its select wire. Picks and Enter reuse that following reader without another peek.
Undoing a source close restores all distinct select destinations, including both the owned peek and other readers.
`open=full` uses the existing zoom path. The dispatcher remains the owner of who may move the keys.

`◀ 2/4 ▶` and PgUp/PgDn call `list.page`. A component's `^name` keys its viewer state; each actor keeps
its own page and occurrence (row id plus group). An agent's place is shown in its tint and never moves the
person's pick, page or focus. Turning a page does not edit the record. On a saved record, layout overrides
such as `[gallery.page::2]` set page size without using `[page::…]`, the note's page address.
The kitchen sink's **peek** section walks the whole interaction on a garden gallery with two pages.

### Proposed split

| Layer | Core (the door owns it) | Extension (a view or plugin adds it) |
|---|---|---|
| shell | stack, status bar, flash + log, theme, unsaved guard, control socket | menu entries |
| tiles | layout tree, focus, entered, zoom, open beside, close, resize, squeeze | tile kinds |
| reader | note surface, header, summary, links, folds, embeds | summary keys per view |
| editing | edit control with completion, draft safety, provenance | completion sources |
| entity nav | up, children, outlinks, backlinks, resources, history, jump | extra projections |
| agents | action registry, arg checking, provenance, `peek` | each extension's actions |
| views | none | board, desk, river, BBS, stats, presence |

Rules that follow from the audit:

- **A view never draws its own tile chrome.** Frames, thumbs, "(e enters)" and zoom come from
  core. Today each view draws them (`dsk:191`, `del:1218`, `riv:270`).
- **Every key a view binds is an action first.** The key is only a binding in the action list.
- **Pane operations use one key layer** across views.
- **Extensions register actions into `ActionSet`s.** They don't intercept keys before the
  shell does.

### Rich component editing (PIE-593)

The reader's edit mode has two views of one draft. The tile menu's **rich edit** opens the drawn view;
**Ctrl+O** switches views. **Tab / Shift+Tab**, **Up / Down**, or a click selects a field.
**Alt+R / Alt+D** add/remove a row; **Alt+V / Alt+X** add/remove a column; the top buttons do the same.
Alt+C remains copy. Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Z and raw editing retain the session's save and undo paths.
The wheel and PageUp/PageDown scroll without moving the cell cursor.

A Markdown table and a static `::graph-table` with `headers: [a, b]` and `rows:` followed by
flow-array rows are editable. Query-backed tables remain service projections; edit their source notes.
Other YAML layouts and malformed components remain available through raw edit. Entering rich edit
does not rewrite source. A cell edit preserves the surrounding source; a graph-table structural edit
rewrites its headers and rows as JSON flow arrays while retaining the other properties.

The door-side adapter contract is exported by `src/surface/element-editor.ts`:

- Register `{ id, read(lines): EditableElement[] }` with `registerElementEditor`; its return value unregisters it.
- Return each element's half-open source-line range and its rows of fields. Each field gives its source line,
  half-open UTF-16 offsets, value, and optionally its own encoder/decoder and escape pattern. A field with
  trailing padding gives `paddingEnd` so typing spaces stays in that field. The component supplies a default encoder.
- `draw(values, width)` calls the component's renderer and reports the field rectangles it drew.
  `drawTableFields` is the shared table renderer adapter.
- `change(op, row, col, to)` returns replacement lines for this element, or null for an unavailable operation.
  It never saves or retains a second buffer. The draft owns selection, history, attribution and incoming patches.

The Markdown and graph-table adapters implement this contract. This is a door-side TypeScript registration
seam, not a new subprocess-extension loading protocol. Adapters read current draft lines on every paint and action,
so an agent's `draft.patch` is immediately editable. Draft owns caret relocation; the rich viewport compensates
for inserted rows above its current cell. Returning to raw uses the same source and caret.

### The reusable editing component

Boundary for a TUI editing component that other TUIs can use:

| Inside | Outside (the host gives it) |
|---|---|
| `Draft`: text, cursor, base revision, dirty, writers | where it is drawn (a `Rect`) |
| edit control: frame, status, hint, keys, `$EDITOR` | where it writes: a target adapter (`src/draft-session.ts`) |
| the draft session: put aside, restore, hold, leave, the agent rule | which actions its keys' commands run |
| completion popup and its keys | completion lookups (`CompletionBoard`) |
| "changed elsewhere" state, keep-draft-to-disk | outline events that mark it |
| provenance: who typed, what the save records | the actor |
| actions: `edit.*`, `complete` | the action registry it registers into |

- Today the frame is in `ed`, the text model in `src/edit.ts`, and completion in `note` and
  `src/comment.ts`. Moving completion into the component closes F9 and gives the board's
  composer completion for free.
- Grapheme width stays a known limit in the editor: it wraps by cells (`wrapRows`), but a click and up/down count one
  cell per character (README); everything else measures in cells (`width`, `graphemes`, PIE-510).

## 6. Ideas from other TUIs (recon, Sep 28)

From a look at shiki, strata and mirador (all MIT). Where each fits:

- **Width tiers** (shiki): side by side, stacked when narrow, focused only when tiny. Hidden
  tiles get zero size, so navigation is the same at every width.
  - Place: tile operations (core). Today: the river has tiers (`riv:243`); lift them into the
    layout tree (PIE-412).
- **Dim unfocused tiles** (mirador).
  - Place: shell theme (core). Today: only the border colour changes (`dsk:191`).
- **Miller columns** (strata): position is depth, preview far right, hidden by width;
  `Alt+←/→` history, `Ctrl+L` jump box.
  - Place: a river view (PIE-431) plus entity navigation. Today: no history anywhere (F5).
- **Dashboard** as weighted rows of panels.
  - Place: a view built on the layout tree (PIE-412). Stats and presence become panels (PIE-430).
- **Theme palette**: ~20 named slots, "reset" inherits the terminal, selection derived from the
  terminal's colours (OSC 10/11).
  - Place: shell (core). Today: 16 VGA colour indices, hard-coded (`src/style.ts:10`).
- **Messages log** keeping every flash, and a **`doctor`** command.
  - Place: shell (core) and the control socket. Today: one 4-second flash (`app:116`); `peek`
    already reports `service.uses` (`app:155`), half of a doctor.

## 7. Tiles: drag, tab, link, preview, layouts (PIE-413, PIE-417, PIE-473, PIE-474)

Completion and snippet key order (PIE-1480): a popup with ready candidates takes Tab to accept, and Esc to dismiss. With no candidates (including a lookup still loading), an active snippet takes Tab/Shift+Tab (`snippet.next`/`snippet.previous`) and Esc (`snippet.end`). With neither, the editor's indentation and cancel behavior stay in effect. Undo during or after a snippet removes the whole insertion. Mouse controls above the draft and `act` use those same actions; agents still need their own draft or its invitation and never select the person's slots.

The bar's `:` and `>` still select screens and actions. If the following characters form `::` or `> [!`, the shared trigger detector returns the prefix to the line and opens completion. An explicit row menu dismisses completion; the bar's selected-note preview stays readable beside the popup.
Groups and mounted screens share tile addressing with the screen and drawer.
With the keys on a group, alt and ^W operate on its focused tile, including
wiring, folding and zooming. The group itself is available through its frame
and menu. Number keys and Tab still navigate the holder unless you enter the
mount. Shell shortcuts (including the drawer toggle and agent panel) keep
their existing host-level meanings.


### A temporary lift (PIE-1189)

`tile.zoom reach=screen` keeps the usual screen zoom (the default). `reach=column`
lifts the tile through its column's full terminal height above the drawer;
`reach=full` fills the terminal above the status bar. The host composition paints
that same tile after the drawer with the desk's existing tile renderer. Its tree,
layout revision and saved layout stay unchanged. There is only one lift.

`^W Z` toggles column reach; `^W F` toggles full reach. The frame's ⇡/⇣ lifts or
drops, and Ctrl+wheel on the frame steps screen → column → full and back.
The keys come from `W_KEYS`, and the frame wheel binding from `ZOOM_WHEEL`.
Typing in the lifted tile keeps it up; work in another tile or the drawer drops it.
Esc closes the tile's own temporary modes first, then drops the lift before the drawer.
Agents may lift another tile, with attribution, but never replace the person's lift.
The showcase's `lift` section exercises this path.

### Layers: floats, the drawer, the lift, menus (PIE-1221)

One order, bottom first, in `layerOrder` (`src/desk/screen-layout.ts`): the screen's tiles, then your drawer and the
screen's floats in the order last touched, then the lift, then menus (a picker such as a tile's menu, a hint card, a
completion list). The drawer is above the floats while the person is in it (a click there, `ctrl+]`); a click on a
float, or leaving the drawer, puts the floats back above. The host layer's `covers` reads that order and the
screen's `drawnAbove` (what its last frame drew as floats and menus): the frame paints the screen's own cells there
over the drawer, and the drawer's mouse hands a click there to the screen, whose hit map already has its floats on
top. Keys go where focus is, as before; the drawer's rows under a float are covered, never moved; a float dragged by
its title keeps moving over the drawer. The showcase's `lift` section floats a tile over the drawer.

Key-clash pass: Z and F were unused after ^W in the shared table used by the desk,
brief, welcome, river, board and showcase. BBS screens have no tile chord; terminal
programs keep their keys while entered. Ctrl+wheel was unused on tile frames;
content wheel handling, including reader and terminal modifiers, is unchanged.


Every completion row draws the same fields (PIE-1478): its label with the typed letters lit, a dim detail on the next line, and its kind at the right edge; the lit row's docs wrap in a panel beside the list when 30 columns are free (`listPreview`'s `fit`, Helix's completion.rs rule), else under it, each sized to what it holds. The editor's `::` popup shows each component once under its schema's group, with the library demo. Enter inserts; Right or `>` opens variations and arguments; Left returns. Inside `{}` and component YAML, schema types select property, query-value, reference, action and enum pickers. The preview reads the schema: its purpose, copyable source, drawing, then props. Property examples and variations use the draft's keys, or the `of=` target's; schema samples apply when there are none. After choosing a row, `y` copies the source; `[copy]` does too; dragging selects and copies the drawing. The `@` preview reads the script note and fence (choose a row then `g`, or click `[g]`, to open its definition beside the draft), a file script's registered path and code, or the extension folder and handler.

### Facets keys (PIE-1220)

In a facets or tags tile, `+` requires the selected key or value, `-` excludes it,
and `0` or Backspace drops the pick (`facets.set`). On a key, Enter or a click on
its name still folds its values; its `[+]` / `[-]` buttons pick the whole key.
`[` gives matches less room and `]` gives them more (`facets.resize`), as the
Matches divider's less/more buttons and drag do. These two keys were unbound in
this tile; other tiles keep their own bindings. The count stays above both lists.

In Matches (PIE-1443) `*` pins or unpins the note (`facets.pin`), `t` asks for a tag or a
property (`facets.tag`), `d` narrows to today and `,` `.` step the day (`facets.day`), and
ctrl+o moves Matches to its next place (`facets.place`). In the `matches` tile `j k` walk
its notes (`facets.match`), Home and `g` go to the first, End and `G` to the last (PIE-1457), ⏎ or space
opens and alt+⏎ opens beside. Home, End, `g` and `G` go to the first and last row of the facets tile too.

### Hint controls (PIE-1096)

Alt+Shift+N (`alt+N`) sets the visible card's stable hint id to status (`hint.never`),
as its [never] control does. No existing key moves: the chord was unbound on every
door screen. It is handled only with a card visible and outside typing modes.
In tune, H switches between values and Hints; H was unbound there. In Hints,
j/k and arrows select, c/s/o set card/status/off, R resets all, and every row and
setting has a click action. These keys belong only to that section; the values
section keeps its existing bindings. All changes use person-only settings actions.


**Picking wires on screen (PIE-1022).** `alt+l` starts link mode for open; `s` changes to select,
`o` to open, `a` to alt. Click a tile, press its number or use `h j k l`; in alt mode an edge or gap
means beside that tile. Esc cancels. The source itself clears that verb. A frame's wire chip and
^W P's **Select shows in**, **⏎ opens in**, **alt opens** rows start this same mode through
`tile.link ask=true verb=…`; the panel's × and `x` clear only that row's wire. No target-name picker.
Frames shorten words to **○ target · ⏎ target · ⤴ target**, then glyphs alone; link mode names the targets
in the hint row and draws a line to connected tiles. A reader's pin chip folds to ▣ (pinned) or □ (not)
when its words do not fit; a click on it pins or lets go, as `p` does.
The old tree preview/target role toggle is gone.

**Key audit.** Plain `s`, `o` and `a` are consumed only while link mode is active, before tile keys.
The tree, links, reader, board and river keep their ordinary bindings outside that mode; editors and terminal
input still take their keys. No new Alt chord. In ^W P only, `x` clears a wire; other rows retain their controls.


Move keys (PIE-943/948): `K/J` are the Mac-terminal fallback for `alt+↑/↓` in readers, river columns,
reply panels and BBS message lists, as in the outline tile. Child rows also take `I/O` to indent/outdent,
`Z` for Move to and `ctrl+z` to undo. Reader `I` (full properties) and `O` (preview) stay unchanged;
floating tiles keep `H/J/K/L` for their position, and drafts, filters and terminal programs keep their keys.
Board lanes and Welcome keep their existing view-order actions. Dropping on a child row nests; its left mark
places the note beside it, without a mouse modifier. River move drags start on that mark so text can still be selected.
Every path sends intent and the read revision to the service, which resolves and moves in one transaction.

`alt+Q` starts/stops a macro (`macro.record`, `macro.stop`); its status-bar chip is the mouse path.
It leaves `q` (back) and `alt+m` (marks) unchanged. A terminal keeps its keys.


The desk is tiles in containers on the layout tree (splits, tab sets and docks, each with its policy, PIE-505). Everything below is an action first (`TILE_ACTIONS` in
`src/desk/tile-actions.ts`, `PANE_ACTIONS`); the key, the drag and `act` call the same code, and an agent's
never takes the person's focus, keys or shown tab.

### Hit areas (Replit's splits, floatty's outer edges)

Tile display titles (PIE-1015) come from `Desk.displayTitle`: tabs, spines, the `%` power-bar scope, peek and the drawer share it as `label`. `layout.get` keeps the frame text in `title` and returns the content label separately in `label`. A reader frame keeps its Work ID, kind, mode and scroll percentage without repeating the title shown by its header; a short frame carries the title when the header cannot. A written title overrides the frame too; switching to the system title restores that frame default. `writtenTitle` and `systemTitle` travel in the saved `TileSpec`; tile addressing names stay unchanged. `tile.title` (`^W n`, "rename this tile…" in the menu, double click the title) is the one rename (PIE-1283): a name-shaped text (letters, digits, `.` `-` `_`) on a tile whose title isn't written separately renames the tile (`rename` in the layout module: what `tile=` and paths such as `who/yo` resolve; refused where its layer has the name) and is the title it shows, any other text (spaces, `{{this.title}}`) writes a title and leaves the name, empty clears a written title; `tile.title.mode` (`^W N`, the frame's name/sys chip, menu) toggles without discarding it. The shared `^W` table had neither n nor N on any desk-hosted screen; no binding moves. Editors, prompts and terminal input still take their own keys first. Interpolation uses outline-core's grammar and property values.

| Where the header is dropped | What happens | Action |
|---|---|---|
| another tile's header, or its centre (the middle ~28% each way) | it joins that tile's tabs, before the tab under the pointer | `layout.move where=tabs index=<n>` |
| one of the four triangles between a tile's diagonals | a split on that side (joins a split already along that axis) | `layout.move where=left/right/up/down` |
| the window's outer left or right two columns, or its bottom row | a full-height column or full-width row beside everything | `layout.move where=edge-left/edge-right/edge-down` |
| its own tab set's side | it leaves the tabs and lands beside them | `layout.move to=<itself> where=…` |

The outer strips are checked first. The top edge is the headers' row, so a full-width row on top is by key
(`^W K`) or `act`. Zones come from the rectangles just placed, on every pointer event (floatty's ghost divider
came from geometry kept from the start of a drag).

### Key map and clash audit

**One key, one meaning** (Evan, Oct 24: "I hit `-` often, I expect 'collapse the current thing to a spine' … then boom, all gone"). A key does the same thing in the drawer, on a screen, in a group and in a float. A place that can't do it says why, or does the nearest thing with the same meaning (a lone drawer tile's `-` folds the drawer to a strip, PIE-1254), never something else. A per-place custom becomes a trap once places share the tile system, so key tests carry drawer and group rows.

X-ray uses bare Ctrl press/release reports from Kitty, a press-only Ctrl toggle,
or `ctrl+]` then `?`. Traditional terminals cannot report bare Ctrl: use the
leader. A terminal tile keeps its existing `ctrl+]` escape and double-press path.
Frame wires use magenta `○ » ^` for select, open and alt-open, with destinations when room permits.
The controls are CP437-native: `◄`/`▼` fold, `≡` menu, `x` close, `◘` float or put back,
and `°`/`•` follow or hold. `«` names the reader a list follows; `*`/`!` name its agent policy.
The frame card names only frame controls. The linking card puts the current wire target first.
`Canvas.float` gives HintCard, ListPicker, completion, the power bar, the keys box and floating composers their raised panel, accent border and half-tone shadow. Theme tokens `float.bg`, `float.border`, `float.shadow` and `backdrop.dim` are tunable through the style cascade; only floats taking keys darken their backdrop.
A frame's menu chip opens the searchable tile menu on click and the controls hint on hover; `?` while the pointer
is on a frame opens that card. Elsewhere `?` opens the keys window (PIE-492, below). Cards
never own keys or focus, and non-choice clicks pass through. Esc dismisses them;
a mode ending or the next action dismisses an unpinned card.


PIE-594: in every reading tile, ↑↓ / j k move by logical block, ( ) by section/fold point,
and [ ] by actionable element. Wheel, PgUp/PgDn, space and Home/End scroll without moving the reading line.
The desk, board, river, detail, welcome, brief, showcase and BBS readers share NoteSurface. Lists,
pickers, menus and the showcase index keep their row navigation; drafts and terminal programs keep their keys.
EchoNet moves message stepping from [ ] to { }, freeing [ ] for the shared reader. Board { } lane-sizing
shortcuts are removed: use ^W < > or drag the border. Unprefixed braces mean message stepping only, wherever bound; ^W { } align the open tiles.
^W [ ] remain tab selection. Raw decoration remains ~ (PIE-707). No other screen binds the changed punctuation.

The focus contract: the screen gives keys to a tile; the reader owns one source place (reading line,
view anchor, edit cursor). Each move updates that place. A click places the reading line, which stays
when focus leaves and dims. Scrolling never moves it; when it is outside the view, ↑↓ resumes at the
view's top. A held edit keeps its cursor and view: focus first, second click places, typing returns to
the cursor. Keys offered as scrolling while outside that edit scroll its draft, never the hidden note.
Only the person's own held drafts dim; an agent's draft is not a held personal edit. Draft previews use
the source map at the cursor, never a fraction of the document's height. Component entry is slice 4.


Comments (PIE-910): `M` cycles shown (trim), full and marks; the byline names the mode and `(M)` and
clicking its count does the same. `m` opens the thread list. Card-header `[–]` and gutter `[–]`/`[+]`
run `thread.toggle`, including whole-note threads. Marks hides every card; one gutter `[+]` can open
just that thread. Returning to shown/full restores the prior expansion state. The gutter stays fixed
outside the content rect during padding, margin, measure and sideways scrolling changes.
No key changed: main-menu `M` makes a screen, `^W M` mounts one, and terminal tiles pass it to their program.

Saved scripts (PIE-900) add `[keys::alt+S]` bindings through `EXT_ACTIONS` and `Dispatcher.pressBinding`,
the same entry used by screen key maps. `Dispatcher.bindingProblem` checks declared action keys and screen
bindings and names the owner of a clash. No built-in key moves. Script bindings are inactive while typing,
in a picker or chord, and in terminals. The scripts showcase reserves `alt+S` for `seed-report`.


Checked against every screen's keys (§4 F4, the board's, the river's, the BBS reader's, the surface's).

| Key | Tiles (the desk and screens on it) | Clash found | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| drag a header's title or tabs | move the tile (tab under the pointer) | none: headers took no drag before | new |
| drag a header's bare line | resize: the border above follows the pointer (`layout.resize`) | the whole header moved the tile | changed |
| `b` | the links of the note in this reader (`links`, a note action): the screen's links tile aims at it, or one opens below the reader with a preview; in a river column its own links under its replies (`column.links`); the board's spec keeps its `b` (`backlinks`, from the lanes) | none in a reader, the surface, the desk, the river, the welcome or the brief (`^W o b` is the art tile, after the prefix); the BBS menu's `b` is a menu letter, not a reader key; the BBS message reader says it has no room and points at `::links` | new; `b` is a reader's own key now (READER_OWN_KEYS), so an extension's line key can't take it |
| `alt+l` | pick its open target; within link mode `s` select, `o` open, `a` alt, then a click, `h j k l` or a number | none (alt keys in use: `alt+b alt+f` history, `alt+c` the board's lanes) | new |
| `alt+z`, `alt+Z` in a reader | route the note shown by the outline's routing rules (`note.route`, PIE-1010); `alt+Z` a dry run. Shell`), as Showcase and Today are |

| an extension action's key on its line (`w` on a `fancy-horror::` line) | while that line (its title or a control) is the reader's current element, run the action (`ext.<id>.<action>`, PIE-512) | any key the reader keeps (`READER_OWN_KEYS`: `[ ] ( ) f F u U r y Y v V i I C c m A X e z j k h l q g G b O / ? n N`, digits, space) or its host keeps (`SurfaceHost.ownKeys`: the BBS reader's `n p t q U`, a desk reader's `p` pin) stays theirs, and only a single printable character binds; the action is still a click on its control or `act` | new, only on an extension's line |
| `^W o l` | a backlinks tile of this tile | `l` is right in `^W h j k l`, but only after `^W o` here, where `l` was free | new, under `^W o` |

| `tab` / `shift+tab`, `←` / `→` while a live tabs figure's tab or row is a reader's `[ ]` position | that figure's next / previous tab (`figure.tab`) | `tab` cycles the desk's tiles; `←` `→` step a river's columns (`tile.focus`, FLOW_KEYS) and a BBS message reader's messages; the board's lanes take `←` `→` but its readers don't; a draft's `tab` indents | the reader claims them only while a figure's element is current (`Pane.claims`, asked before the desk's tab and a kind's keys); `esc` lets go of it and they're the host's again |
| `↑` / `↓` in a live figure or inline links list | select the previous / next row, staying at the boundary until Esc; Enter opens the row | elsewhere the reading line steps logical blocks | one inline `RowView`, including rows selected before repaint |
