| `End`, `G` in a reader (the desk's, detail, preview, the BBS reader, the showcase's) | the last line to the bottom edge; pressed again, past the end to the middle (`scroll to=end`, `reader.overscroll`, PIE-622); `Home` back to the top | `G` is Goodbye on the main menu only, a screen of its own; no reader, desk tile, board or river bound it (`READER_OWN_KEYS` already kept it for the reader); `End` in a draft stays the line's end, in a list the last row | new: `G`, and End's second press |

Inside a terminal tile every key is the program's (`^W` included: vim's window keys work), so the only door
key there is `ctrl+]`. Inside a mount (a board or river tile, any screen, a part or a group, PIE-651) its own keys work (a brief tile is a reader of the brief kind: `,` `.` are its own); `1`–`9`, `V`, Tab, alt and `^W` stay the
desk's, until `^W e` goes in: then every key is the mount's, its `^W` too, and `ctrl+]` (or an Esc with nothing left to close in it) comes out.

### The keys window and the keymap (PIE-492)

- **One keymap file:** `keybindings.json` in the door's state dir (`EP0CH_KEYMAP` for a test), the outliner's shape: an
  act line to its keys, `[]` unbinds (`{"tile.close": ["ctrl+w q"], "element.select by=1": ["n"]}`). A key is one key
  or a ^W chord by its one name (`chordName`). The door never writes the outliner's file: it refuses ids it doesn't know.
- **What follows it:** every key table (`effective`: W_KEYS through `wNow`, each mode's, HYPER_KEYS, a screen spec's
  keys), so the match, the ^W panel, the tile menu's keycap and the hint row say the same key. A key matched in code
  (the reader's `z`, the shell's alt+v) follows through two seams: the App's keymap layer presses an action for a key
  the file gives it (`layerEntry`, never while typing or in a terminal tile), and `ActionSet.call` refuses the first
  action a typed key runs when the file moved that key away (`moved`), saying where it went.
- **The keys window** (`keys` tile kind and screen): on a screen of tiles `keys.open` (`?`) opens it as a tile beside
  the focused one (`Desk.openKeys`, PIE-1425), so it resizes, docks and closes like any tile and gives the keys back to
  its opener; beside a tile that halves under `LIST_COLS` (40) it opens below, and under a tile already narrower along
  the bottom edge at the screen's width (`Desk.sides`, the one order for a list that wants width; the trail tile asks it with its own 18 columns). It opens filtered to where you
  are: the scope the focused tile says its keys run in now (`Pane.keysScope`: a reader's `note`, a river column's
  `river` unless an edit is open), else its kind's action set's; the keys screen is for a screen without tiles (`ep0ch --screen keys`, a BBS list). `⏎` (or a second click
  on the selected row) is `keys.run`: the binding's action, pressed as the person in the tile the window opened beside;
  one needing arguments the binding doesn't give opens its form in the power bar (`bar.form op=open`). `esc` and `q`
  close it. The catalog's bindings filtered as typed (`in:<scope>` is where a binding works, the catalog's `worksIn`: a
  scope exactly, a set a table says it is part of, `KeyTable.within`: `in:river` holds the river column's tables, and
  the ^W chords on a screen of tiles), each row one sentence: the action's
  `words`, then where or when, or the row's own `says` (`TableRow.says`, `KeyRow.says`) where its hint word alone would
  be a fragment (`test/key-rows.test.ts` refuses a `, <word>` tail and an act argument), each with its layer, what it takes its key from and what takes it first, and whether a terminal tile's
  program still gets it. `r` records the new key (^W then a key for a ^W binding), says what it would take before ⏎
  saves it; `u` unbinds, `d` gives the built-in keys back, `ctrl+z` undoes. Its own keys are a table too.
- **Conflicts are real fights only** (PIE-1425), by the order a key is looked up: two rows of one key table on one key
  whose kinds meet (`only`/`unless`: the table is read first to last, so the second never runs), and one key in two
  entries of the keymap file (the App presses the last). An action set's own keys are one key, one meaning at a time
  (its code picks by state: esc clears the bar, then closes it), and a key in two layers is a shadow said on each row,
  only where both work (PIE-1439): a key row that is one screen's own says so (`KeyRow.on`: the main menu's letters
  are `on: "menu"`), two screens' own keys never meet, a screen without tiles has no tile, mode or ^W keys, and a
  screen's spec keys come before a tile's (`Desk.boundKey`), so the river's `x` is its own. At 40 columns rows that
  read alike once cut keep the word that tells them apart (`ellipsizeApart`), and the hint row says esc early.
  A row of an action's keys reads as a whole sentence: the action's words, then the row's where or when.
- **Unbound** (the fourth tab): every action of every action set that no binding reaches (`unbound` in the catalog,
  read from `allActionSets`, never a list), by layer and scope; its `r` is the same rebind, through the App's keymap
  layer (`layerEntry`) for one key or the ^W table's `wNow` for a chord, so it says first what that key does now.
- **Not yet rebindable alone:** a table binding several keys under one name with different meanings (^W h j k l, the
  hyper digits) and a key after another key (`a then c`): the window says why on the row.

**Key clash audit (`?`):** the desk's `?` with the pointer on a frame is that frame's hint card; anywhere else it is
the keys window, filtered to the focused tile, at once (PIE-1425: with the hint row cut, a first `?` used to show only
the rest of the row, and read as `?` doing nothing; `keys.more` is the `? more` chip's click now). The main menu's `?` was
the help screen and is now the keys window; help moved to `H` there (a letter no menu item has), and the menu's key line says `? keys H help`. Every other screen had no
`?`; the App opens the window there. `^W ?` (the ^W list) and `ctrl+] ?` (x-ray) don't move. In a filter, a draft or a
terminal tile `?` is typed, as before.

### The hyper layer (PIE-699)

⌃⌥⇧⌘ held together is **hyper** (Kitty keyboard protocol modifiers 15; Raycast's Hyper Key, Karabiner and Hammerspoon
make Caps Lock send it). Nothing else uses that namespace, so the door can take it before the focused tile's own keys: a
hyper chord works while typing in a draft, a filter or a terminal tile, where a bare key is text or the program's.
It is optional (off by default: `EP0CH_HYPER=1`, which wins either way, or `ep0ch act hyper.set on=true`, kept in the
state dir as `hyper.json`), and every chord is a duplicate: the same action has a key outside it, a click and `act`.
The keymap is one file, `src/hyper.ts` (`HYPER_KEYS`); this table is checked against it (test/hyper.test.ts).

| Chord | Action | Other ways |
|---|---|---|
| `✦p` | `bar.open`, the power bar | `ctrl+k`, `cmd+k`, `^K` on the status bar |
| `✦g` | `bar.open scope=screens` | `ctrl+k` then `@` |
| `✦h` `✦j` `✦k` `✦l` | `tile.focus dir=left/down/up/right` | `^W h j k l`, a click, Tab |
| `✦1` `✦2` `✦3` `✦4` `✦5` `✦6` `✦7` `✦8` `✦9` | `tile.focus tile=#n` | `1`–`9`, a click |
| `✦-` `✦=` | `tile.collapse on=true/false` | bare `-` `+` `=`, `alt+h`, `^W c`, the frame card's fold row, a click on a spine |
| `✦z` | `tile.zoom` | `^W z` |
| `✦u` | `tile.zoom reach=column`, lift or drop the focused tile's column | `^W Z` |
| `✦a` | `agent.lift`, `tile.zoom` as a column on the agent tab you're using (a preset: the one you last typed in or clicked, an agent from `alt+g` too, `tile=role:agent`, alias `drawer.agent`, wherever the tile grammar finds it; one on a screen not shown comes into the drawer first, and with none open a new agent tab opens); again drops it | a status-bar button (`::button{label="▲ lift" act="agent.lift"}`), `act` |
| `✦n` | `note.new` | `ctrl+n` |

**What passes through what.** Checked in a real pane with injected bytes (a physical keyboard, macOS and ssh from a
laptop are not verified here: run `keys.probe` and press the chord to see what yours sends):

| Layer | Result |
|---|---|
| The door | reads `CSI 107;16u` (and `CSI 107:75;16u`, caps lock `CSI 107;80u`, and xterm's `CSI 27;16;107~` from tmux) as `hyper+k` with the layer on; as `super+K` with it off. Without the Kitty keyboard protocol (the terminal never answered `CSI ? u`, as tmux doesn't) no modifier chord of this kind exists to read |
| A terminal tile | with the layer on, the decoder keeps a hyper report from the tile's program; every other report goes through unchanged (`test/hyper.test.ts`) |
| Herdr 0.9.3 | passes `CSI 107;16u` through to a pane's program intact (a private `herdr --session`, bytes injected into its input, a pane program that asked for the protocol with `CSI > 5 u`), and rewrites xterm's `CSI 27;16;107~` to `CSI 107;16u`. It binds no hyper chord by default (its default keys are `prefix+…`, with `ctrl+v` for remote image paste and plain letters only in its navigate mode), so nothing is swallowed unless `config.toml` binds one: `ep0ch doctor` names any such key (`hyper keys`), and `herdr server reload-config` applies the change. Herdr's `cmd+k` is its own only if you bind it |
| ssh | a pipe: the bytes arrive as sent; what matters is the local terminal |
| The outer terminal | must send the Kitty keyboard protocol with super (kitty, Ghostty and WezTerm document it; Terminal.app does not) and must not claim the chord itself; macOS terminals may keep `cmd` chords for their own menus, so check with `keys.probe` |

### Tiles and their actions

`tile=` names the tile an action is for. Each action has one name.

| Action | Args | Keys, mouse |
|---|---|---|
| `layout.get` | | `peek` shows the same `tree` |
| `layout.list`, `screen.save`, `layout.load` | `name` | `^W r`, `^W w`, `alt+d` |
| `layout.move` | `tile=<tile>`, `to`, `where`, `index` | drag a header, `^W m t T H J K L` |
| `tile.open` (`pane.split`: `kind`, `dir`) | `kind`, `name`, `cmd`, `file`, `source`, `note`, `cwd`, `to`, `where` | `^W o`, `^W O` |
| `tile.close`, `tile.focus`, `tile.info` | `tile=<tile>` | `^W x`, the board's `x`; click, Tab, 1-9 |
| `tile.resize`, `tile.zoom` | `by`, `axis`; `on` | `^W < > + -`; `^W z` |
| `tile.float`, `tile.collapse` | none; `on` | `^W f`, the board's `o`, the frame card's put-back row; `^W c`, the board's `c`, `alt+c` |
| `tile.widen` | `tile=<tile>` | `^W W`, a click on a flow column's spine |
| `tile.link` | `tile=<tile>`, `to` (a tile, or a path across an edge: `group/tile`, `../tile`, `@drawer/tile`, `@screen/tile`; none unlinks), `verb=select\|open\|alt` (default open), `remove=true` removes one select target | `alt+l` starts open; `s` select, `a` alt; frame chips and ^W P start the same mode |
| `tile.dock` | `on`, `edge`, `container` | `^W p`; the policy panel's edge row; the board's `T` `B`; `p` while dragging; a drop on a dock's handle; a click on a header's `⇤ docked` (undocks) |
| `tile.slide` | `open` | `^W d`, a handle's click, `Esc` in an open dock; it shuts when the keys leave |
| `layout.lock` | `on` | no key (PIE-1346); a click on the hint row's lock chip or the drawer frame's; the policy panel's locked row on the screen; the power bar's row ("lock the screen", "unlock your drawer": the layout the keys are in) |
| `layout.gather` | `side=left\|right\|centre\|sides`, optional `node=<container id>`; default: tile's row/flow. Sets spines=start/end/centre/book | `^W { } \|`, Container menu, `^W P` |
| `layout.policy` | `node`, the policy's fields, `clear`; nothing: a read | `^W P`, `⏎` or a click on a row |
| `tile.agent` | `tile=<tile>`, `policy` (free, edit, off, inherit; none cycles) | `^W g`, a click on the tile's agents chip, the tile's `⋯` menu row; the screen's default is `layout.policy agents=` and a row in `^W P` |
| `keys.more` | none (the person's view; an agent's is refused) | a click on the hint row's `? more` |
| `keys.probe` | `on` (default toggles); the person's: an agent's is refused | the power bar's `>` scope, a click on the hint row's `✦ hyper` chip, `act` |
| `hyper.set` | `on` | `ep0ch act hyper.set on=true`, the power bar's `>` scope; `EP0CH_HYPER=1` wins |
| `tile.preview` | `tile=<tile>`, `where` (right, down, left, up; default by `splitAxis`) | `^W v` beside, `^W V` below, `O` in a reader; a click on `v` or `V` in the `^W` chord's row |
| `tile.type`, `tile.restart` | `text` | typing in the tile, `⏎` on an exited one |
| `tab.select` | `by` (1, -1) | a click on a tab, `alt+n alt+p`, `^W [ ]` |

### The river: focus is not the layout

Evan's notes (Sep 29): a click just to focus a column shifted the whole strip, and the column he'd been reading
collapsed to its headings. So the river keeps two things apart:

- **Focus** is which column has the keys (glossary: focus). A click in a column, `h` `l`, `tab`, and `back`
  move it and its highlight only; every column's place, width, cover and scroll stay cell for cell. A column
  off the strip altogether is the one exception: `h` `l` bring it on, one step.
- **The wide column** is what the layout is built around (`describe()`'s `wide`). Only an explicit shift
  moves it: `w`, a click on a column's **header** (its top border; anywhere on a spine, which is all title strip, as a click on a board spine opens it), the `widen` action,
  and an open that couldn't otherwise show the new column full. The column the person was reading (the one
  they were in before) stays full beside it when there's room; a hold (`p`) outranks it.
- **Why these inputs:** the first click must be harmless, so the shift needs a different target, not a second
  click on the same place (a second click on a card already opens it, and a double click selects a word). A
  header is the tile grammar's place for acting on the whole tile (§7's drag). `w` is free on every screen's
  reading keys (the backlinks dock's `w` and the step choice's `w` only live while those are open; the desk's
  is under `^W`); `z` was the obvious zoom key but it's the surface's unfold.
- **Covered, not collapsed:** a peek column draws the same view a full column does, at the same reading width,
  and shows its first cells; its right-hand neighbour lies over the rest like a dock, with the board's
  dock shadow (`▒`) on the edge, and the visible part is dimmed (`Canvas.dim`). Nothing rewraps when it
  widens. Only the far columns become spines. Since PIE-515 this is the desk's drawing of any flow's peek: the
  river's columns are tiles in a flow on the desk, not a strip of their own.
- **The wide column is never a peek** (PIE-1392): narrower than a full column (a 40-column pane), it is laid out at
  the width it has, as a desk tile is, and the others give way to spines. A column folded with `tile.collapse` (`^W c`,
  neat's two up) stays a spine whatever the room; the wide place passes to the nearest open column, and `^W W` opens it.
- **One click for its note:** a river column draws its note through the surface's digest and hands a click on any of
  its elements (a button, a step, a list row, a link) to the surface's own `pressLink`, the reader's click: one
  decision, not a second copy in the column.
- **Opens shift as little as they can:** ⏎ on a card or a followed link adds the column after its source as
  before. If that column already shows full next to the source, nothing moves; otherwise the new column
  becomes wide and the source stays full beside it. A close hands a full column's place to the column
  sliding into the gap. A click opens a card only in the column that already has the keys.
- **Back and forward** in the river go between columns (`SurfaceHost.history`): back gives the keys to the
  column this one was opened (or last reached) from, forward returns; each widens its target only when it's
  covered. Keys and mouse as in every reader (alt+← alt+→, backspace, the side buttons, the `← back` row
  under the column's title). An agent's `back` is refused: it would move the person's keys.

### The welcome screen (a spec on the desk)

The menu's `C` opens it (it pinned `[[claude-now]]` before; that page is now the welcome's fallback). Its keys,
checked against the desk's, the surface's and the other screens':

| Key | Welcome | Clash found | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| `1`–`9`, `0` | pick the welcome note on that tab (`0` the tenth) | the desk's `1`–`9` focus a tile | the tabs take them here (its spec's `digits: false`, and `1`–`9` `0` in its key map): the headers don't number the tiles, and focus is `Tab`, a click or `^W h j k l`; `act tile=` names them (welcome, detail, backlinks, preview). After `alt+l` a digit is still the desk's (the tile to link) |
| `Tab` | list → detail → backlinks → preview | none: the desk's reading order | the backlinks sit under the detail they belong to and the preview runs the full height, so reading order is the useful order; giving a tile the keys never moves a tile |
| `alt+↑` `alt+↓` in the list, a note dragged up or down the list | put it up or down the Welcome view's hand-set order (`welcome.move`; the first move makes the view, `[query::welcome]`) | the board's lanes use the same keys and drag for the same thing (`card.reorder`); a reader binds none | the same gesture for the same order (#190's), in the list only |
| `L` | the next logo (`welcome.logo`) | the board's `L` moves a card right, not on this screen; the surface binds no `L` | new, this screen only |
| `⏎` on a link | opens it in the preview (the detail's link) | none: the desk's link rule | kept; with nothing picked yet, `⏎` takes the first element (`]` then `⏎`) |
| `alt+⏎`, ctrl-click, alt-click | read it in the detail | everywhere else it opens beside (a new reader) | the same chord, "open fresh": on a one-note screen, fresh is the detail itself; back returns. In the preview with no link picked, `alt+⏎` (or a click on its `⇱ read here`) reads the preview's own note in the detail |
| `p` in the detail | refused, said | the desk's readers hold or follow with `p` | the detail never follows here |
| `j k ⏎ s K w h n .` in the backlinks | the board dock's keys | none (the tile has the keys only when focused) | kept, one drawing; landing on the tile (Tab, a click) shows its selected row in the preview, as the board's dock shows its first on opening |
| `q` | the menu | none | kept |
| `Esc` | closes the innermost temporary thing; with none, stays and says `nothing to close · q leaves` | it went to the menu | changed: Esc never leaves a screen (§1, "Esc") |

### The home base (a spec on the desk)

Bare `ep0ch` where nothing names an outline (src/home.ts, `homeSpec`): one tile of its own kind (`home`), the logo
over a RowView list of this machine's outlines and the machines opened from here; its choices are `HOME_ACTIONS`,
and what one needs typed or picked is a list picker over the screen (`DeskApi.overlay`). It runs in a door that is on
no outline yet (`homeBase` in src/door.ts: `Ctx.home` is how a choice ends it); opened by name elsewhere it makes,
connects and opens nothing. Its policy keeps the whole screen (`host: none`), and its digits are its own (none bound).
More sections (running agents, switching outlines from a sidebar) are tiles a later spec adds beside it.

| Key | Home base | Clash found | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| `j k ↑ ↓`, the wheel | the cursor (`home.pick`) | none: every list's | kept |
| `⏎`, a click | the row's choice: open, connect, new, import, add | none | a click chooses at once, as the menu's do: a launcher, not a reader |
| `n` `i` `a` `x` `r` | new, import, add a machine, forget it, reload | the desk binds none of them bare; a tile's own keys come first | this tile only |
| `q` | quit (nothing opened) | the desk's leave | kept: the only screen, so leaving it ends the home base |
| `esc` | closes a picker over it; with none, stays and says `nothing to close · q leaves` | it quit the door | changed: Esc never leaves (§1, "Esc") |

### What's not done here

- **Screen notes in the outline:** done for the screens people make (PIE-565): `screen.save` writes one, every door
  registers them (`src/desk/screen-notes.ts`). The built-in screens are still specs in code (`src/desk/screen-specs.ts`).
- **One river:** done (PIE-515). The River is a spec on the desk: a flow of `river.column` tiles (`src/river/column.ts`),
  the engine's squeeze, calm focus, explicit shift, opens into the next column, back and forward and held
  columns. The `river` layout is that flow beside a preview following the column with the keys.
- **The board** is a screen spec on the desk (PIE-515, gap 3): its lanes are query tiles in a `columns` container
  filled from the hub (`hub:<id>`), its docks and floats the desk's, saved as a layout in `delivery.json`;
  a board saved by name is a screen note (PIE-565). The `board` layout still puts
  the whole board screen in a tile (`ScreenTile`), a preview tile after its card.
- **The host layer's tabs: your drawer (PIE-498).** The host layer holds the drawer's desk: its agent tabs and any tile put in (`tile.drawer`, `^W a`, a drop on the chip or the open drawer, a screen left with terminals running), kept across screens and restarts. Still to come: the admin outline and detail as default tabs, and the drawer from another edge.
- **Where the keys are** (focus) is the person's, an input to the screen-layout module (`ctx.person`), and each operation
  answers where they go after it. The board's own moves between its areas (the lanes, the preview, the details) set
  them directly; PIE-514 makes the keys and "typing in" a shell-owned query.
- **Policy rows that don't act yet:** `collapsible` acts on docks and on a tile folding to a spine (`tile.collapse`); a
  split or tab set folding whole is still to come (a flow's columns squeeze themselves, PIE-513); `accepts` checks a tile's kind against the containers it would join, and a registry entry's
  `accepts.tiles` checks a tab set's.
- **Terminal keys:** while the person is in a terminal tile the bytes go through raw (F-keys, modified arrows,
  Insert, bracketed pastes); only mouse reports and `ctrl+]` are the door's.


### Run-again key audit (PIE-957)

One key for "run it again, read it again" on every door screen: bare **`r`**. It is that already in a reader (`projection.refresh`:
tickets, files, a handler line, a pending `@ask`), the query, facets, activity, Who and Welcome lists (`*.reload`), the board
(`board.reload`) and a comment session. The three places it means something else, and what S7 decided:

| Where | `r` is | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| the EchoNet message reader | reply (`echo.reply`) | stays: an EchoNet copy isn't an outline note (the reader's `R` reply refuses it), and it has no projection to run again; its `ownKeys` already keeps `r` from extensions. Settle with the EchoNet screen when it takes `R` |
| a blank tile's chooser | picks "reader" | stays: a letter in a menu of kinds, not a command (a chooser takes its letters first) |
| the Herdr Tree (the outliner's console) | restore a selected Trash root | reveal source moves from `Shift+R` to `alt+r`, matching the door (PIE-995); the earlier audit recorded `r`. The shared `tree.current.reveal` action supplies keys, menu/click and hints. `alt+shift+r` still reveals the first authored reference |

Herdr Detail keeps `Shift+R` for source reveal and `alt+r` for writing history; its annotation and Trash modes keep their own `r` actions. No door screen changes bindings in PIE-995.

Bare `r` in a reader with no `[ ]` position on something it can run refuses and lists the candidates (S6 binds that refusal to `RUN_AGAIN_KEY`
in `src/projection.ts`); the gutter mark beside a generated region offers "run again" as the same action, `projection.refresh {block, line}`,
by click. `alt+r` stays reveal, `R` reply, `ctrl+z` the undo chain (PIE-957: layout changes join it).

### Replies key audit (PIE-707)

On every NoteSurface reader (desk reader, preview, river column, BBS message and EchoNet reader),
`R` opens a reply; raw decoration moves from `R` to `~`. Lowercase `r` keeps its existing refresh
behavior; the EchoNet reader keeps its existing `r` reply action (see the run-again audit below). `alt+r` still reveals in the tree;
`alt+R` still opens the restart drawer. Reader `t` opens the thread drawer, while the board's `t`
opens the tree only outside a reader. `T` opens the full thread list from the drawer.

Outside readers no bindings move: the main menu's `R` reads, the home base/Who/query/facet/waiting
lists use `r` to reload, Tune's `R` reverts, a blank tile's `r` chooses a reader, and the layout
prefixes retain their `r`. Comment sessions keep `r` for annotation replies. Editors type ordinary
letters. A saved screen's existing key map can bind `note.reply`, `note.quote` and `note.thread`;
they are ordinary NOTE_ACTIONS, with mouse menu entries and `act` access.

A reply starts with `re:: <message title>` (queryable front matter). Save creates a child stamped
`replyTo` and the source's `thread`, then closes the split. Esc discards, with the existing second-Esc
confirmation after typing. The source keeps its reading place and scrolls with the wheel or Page keys
while the editor keeps its cursor. A selected passage becomes a frozen quote callout with its block
reference; an existing paragraph anchor is used when present. Quotes do not rewrite the source.
Replies are notes; whole-note comments and patch notes keep their existing behavior.

### Moving a note key audit (PIE-943)

`note.move`, `tree.move` and `column.move`: one set of keys, the same in every list of notes. Audited against every screen.

| Key | Meaning here | Every other use, and why they don't meet |
|---|---|---|
| `alt+↑` `alt+↓` in a reader (desk reader, detail, preview, welcome and board readers, BBS message reader), the outline tile, a river column | the note shown, the selected row or card, one place up or down among its siblings | The board's lanes and the Welcome list put a card up or down a view's hand-set order (`card.reorder`, `welcome.move`): the same gesture on a different order, in the tile that owns the cursor. A terminal tile passes them to its program; an edit, comment or picker takes its keys first. Nothing else reads them |
| `alt+←` `alt+→` | not a move key anywhere | Readers and columns: back and forward (`historyKey`, `tile.travel`). The outline tile binds neither |
| `K` `J` in the outline tile | up, down: the fallback for a terminal (a Mac's) that doesn't send Option+arrows | The tree binds `j k` to pick; a floating tile uses `H J K L` to move itself (`float.place`, the desk takes them first); the reader keeps `J K` for sizing in a mode |
| `I` `O` in the outline tile | in under the note above, out after its parent | The reader's `I` (full properties) and `O` (open a preview) are readers' only; the tree binds neither |
| `Z` in a reader, the outline tile, a river column, the reply panel or BBS message list | ask where it goes | The selected child row moves in a list; otherwise the reader’s note moves. `>` was the first choice and is the board screen's widen-tile and the power bar's actions prefix; `P` picks a follower's source and steps the BBS list; `m` is the thread list |
| `ctrl+z` in a reader or the outline tile | puts the last move of that note back | In a reader it joins the chain (callout, image, step, extension, move: whichever came last). In the tile only for a row with a move newer than the last trash; otherwise the desk's untrash |
| a drag in the outline tile | onto a row: under it (first); onto its `▸` mark at the left edge: above it (it started below) or below it | A press on a row selects; double-click opens. A match of a view's row doesn't drag (it's a copy) and says so; a drop outside the tile or inside the note itself moves nothing |


### Sources inspector keys (PIE-1100)

`S` or the header's Sources control opens and enters the inspector; a click anywhere in it also
enters it. `Esc` closes Sources from either the inspector or the reader beside it. The tile hint row
reads its bindings from the mode key table. The panel's footer only names who has the keys and is
clickable to transfer them; `sources.focus on=true|false` is the same action. The back arrow or Left
returns to the source list; `q` or `S` inside also closes the inspector.

Inside it, `j k`, arrows and Tab select rows; PgUp/PgDn, space and the wheel scroll. Text wraps and
a drag selects and copies through the reader's selection part. Result rows select by click, open
with Enter and open beside with alt+Enter, using the reader's usual navigation. `g` reveals the
figure's line in its host note, or opens a separate transform note when the source has one.

Enter or `e` on a figure property opens the shared inspector field. Density uses schema values;
group and column choices come from the result rows' property names. Space toggles multiple columns.
Enter or [save] writes through `draft.patch`, attributed as the actor; ctrl+z undoes it once.
Query checks warn after capture. Esc or [cancel] keeps its typing for the next edit. The tune inspector uses the same
field with `e` or Enter and keeps its existing nudge/save behavior. Other screens' keys are unchanged.

### Sources downloads (PIE-956)

Inside the Sources inspector only, `c` downloads the selected source as CSV and `J` as JSON.
Both have clickable rows and `sources.download format=csv|json source=<number> [path=<new file>]`.
The default destination is the door's outline state `downloads/` directory; the action reports the path.
These keys were unbound in Sources; other reader and screen bindings do not move. Enter opens a source,
then Enter on a download row runs the same action. The published Sources panel uses the same `site.answer`
and row serialization, with native file downloads and read-only parameters.

### Library selection (PIE-1025)

The library tile uses the reader’s `Gesture`, `Selection`, `selectText` and `paintRange`
(`src/surface/selection.ts`), and the same `Ctx.copy` / OSC 52 path. Source gutters are
drawn decoration, excluded from copied text. Drag selects and copies; double click
selects a word, triple click a line. The library showcase demonstrates all three.

`y` and the `copy` control keep copying the whole variation source. `cmd+c` copies
the selected text, as in readers and editors; no other screen’s keys move.
`library.select text=` returns matching drawn words without moving the person’s
selection or writing their clipboard.

### Inline list keys (PIE-1035)

Every NoteSurface reader (detail, preview, river, board, BBS and welcome) uses the
same inline RowView for views, `::links`, `::backlinks`, live tables and tabs.
Down/up and j/k step into the list, select rows, and leave at its ends. Enter opens
and alt+Enter opens beside; a click selects and previews. The wheel targets the
list under the pointer and passes to the note at its limits. The box edge shows a
scroll thumb. Page keys still scroll the note. Screen, editor and picker bindings
do not change; their existing precedence applies.

The shared part is `src/surface/inline-list.ts`, lent by NoteSurface to the existing
Doc and embed renderers. Rows remain reader elements and use `element.select`,
`element.open` and `element.scroll`. The `inline-lists` showcase demonstrates 60 rows.

### Empty screen and drawer home (PIE-1119)

Closing the last tile is a screen-layout `close` operation. Its empty tab set is saved and restored as empty.
The screen host draws its home with the shared document renderer: the power bar button, New note, Shell and
Agent buttons (`::button`) and a `::graph-keys` figure. Nothing starts by itself; alt+s always opens a shell.
The empty agent panel uses those same controls. A new note in an empty reader or detail opens its draft in
that tile, including a tile in the drawer.

A mode's key hints belong in its tile footer once. The drawer edge and the status line show state and controls;
they do not repeat the tile's mode hints. An explicit keys figure can teach the home or empty panel's first steps.

A plain click on the drawer agent chip opens and closes the drawer (the most frequent click: never anything else); alt+click or a right click opens `host.agent`'s picker, "a new agent tab". Choosing starts the pick in a new tab and
ends nothing (PIE-1331): only ^W x on a tab ends its program. The picker includes the folder and this-outline/every-outline scope. An
`EP0CH_DAILY_AGENT` override is named on the chip, explained in the picker and doctor, with the shell line to
remove. `ep0ch agent use <name> [--everywhere]` uses that same action.

### Proposal resolution (PIE-1085)

An open proposal's reader or selected proposal embed owns `r` for `proposal.resolve`; otherwise the reader's
`r` remains projection refresh. A proposal row in waiting-on-you uses `r` to resolve; a held request row uses
it to send. Both mouse controls and the power bar call the same proposal action. The service's proposal router
runs the tidy responder, routes edits through its existing draft/saved comparison, and records the extension
undo receipt. No second reader, editor, model client, or rules engine.

### Placement (PIE-1187)

`placement()` in `screen-layout.ts` owns where an open lands. Desk entry points apply its open operation once.
The destination keeps its id, name and place. A replaced note in a reader no list feeds is born folded toward the invoking tile,
newest nearest the destination; unrelated readers keep their shape. Only the person's open (including a pressed
script) moves the person's keys or the outline's cursor. An unpressed script, rule or agent changes only its destination. Wires use tile ids
across desks and survive grouping, drawer moves and renames. `room` squeezes flows; it never folds ordinary readers.
